\displaystyle\geq 1-O\big(\exp\{-c_{0,2}k\}\big),

(E.5)

for some constants
c_{0,1},c_{0,2}>0
.

Proof of
Lemma

E.1
.

See
Section

E.5
.
∎

For fixed
s^{\star}
, we collect all the indices
r
such that the corresponding nice event
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,r}
will be involved in the coming analysis. Define
S(s^{\star})=\big\{s^{\star}-1,s^{\star}-\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ odd}\},2s^{\star},4s^{\star}\big\}
. And we will stick to the following high probability event

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{0}\coloneqq{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\infty}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\sharp}\cap(\cap_{r\in S(s^{\star})}{\mathcal{E}}_{0,r}).

(E.6)

With Lemma
E.1
, we have that
\Pr({\mathcal{E}}_{0})\geq 1-O(k^{-c_{0}})
for some constant
c_{0}>0
.

Preparation for characterizing one-step gradient

Following the same manner as the proof for the non-sparse case, we first characterize the alignment of the gradient step (without adversarial error term
\mathrm{err}_{m,l,i}^{(t)}
). We begin with the definition of a minimal setup, that collects all the essential elements to form the one-step gradient. The following definition is the sparse analogue of
Definition

D.2
. Apart from the method of generating the noise, in the sparse case, we will analyze the gradient in a coordinate-wise manner to adapt to the sparse structure.

Definition E.2
.

Fix
k
-sparse vectors
\theta,\theta^{\star}\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
with
\phi=\mathsf{supp}(\theta)
and
\phi^{\star}=\mathsf{supp}(\theta^{\star})
. Let
\rho=\langle{\theta},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle
. Suppose that a single batch of data
\{(z_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
is i.i.d. generated from
{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}
. We fix the index
m
as the current neuron. We first sample
\phi_{m,1},\phi_{m,2},\ldots\phi_{m,L}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}{\text{Unif}}({\mathcal{S}}_{k,m})
, i.e., uniform distribution over all
k
-sparse supports with
m
-th index always included. Given these random supports, we sample independent noises
\xi_{m,l}\sim{\text{Unif}}({\mathbb{S}}^{k-1}(\phi_{m,l}))
for
l\in[L]
. Now, for each
l\in[L]
and
\gamma=o(1)
, we define
w_{m,l}=(\gamma\theta+\xi_{m,l})/\|\gamma\theta+\xi_{m,l}\|_{2}
. Then our target is

\displaystyle{\widebar{g}}_{m}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{nL}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\big(\psi(y_{i},\langle{w_{m,l}},{z_{i}}\rangle)\cdot z_{i}-\widehat{\psi}_{1}(y_{i})\cdot w_{m,l}\big).

(E.7)

The associated good event is defined as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\{\sup_{l,j}|\langle{\xi_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|\leq\epsilon\};

(E.8)

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{m}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\max\big\{\sup_{l\neq l^{\prime}}|\phi_{m,l}\cap\phi_{m,l^{\prime}}|,\sup_{l}|\phi_{m,l}\cap\phi^{\star}|,\sup_{l}|\phi_{m,l}\cap\phi|\big\}\leq\log k\Big\}.

(E.9)

Almost orthogonality

Recall that our perturbation noise
\xi_{m,l}
is sampled from
{\text{Unif}}({\mathbb{S}}^{k-1}(\phi_{m,l}))
, which is approximately isotropic. One can presume that each
\xi_{m,l}
is evenly distributed among different coordinates. Additionally, we expect that
(\xi_{m,l},\phi_{m,l})
and
(\xi_{m,l^{\prime}},\phi_{m,l^{\prime}})
should have a negligible overlap. These two qualitative properties, which can help simplify the analysis, are captured by Eq. (
E.8
) and Eq. (
E.9
) in
Definition

E.2
. The following lemma characterizes the property of the perturbed weights
w_{m,l}
on the nice event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)\cap\widetilde{{\mathcal{E}}}_{m}
.

Lemma E.3

(Polarized weight on a nice event, sparse case)
.

Consider the setting in
Definition

E.2
with
\gamma<1/2
. Suppose that the nice event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}
holds and
\|\theta^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq 1/\log k
, then we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{l,j}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq 2(\gamma|\theta_{j}|+\epsilon);

(E.10)

\displaystyle\sup_{l}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq 2(\gamma|\rho|+\epsilon).

(E.11)

Additionally, we have that

\displaystyle\sup_{l\neq l^{\prime}}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{w_{m,l^{\prime}}}\rangle|\leq 4(\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log k).

(E.12)

Proof of
Lemma

E.3
.

See
Section

E.5
.
∎

This lemma, serving as the counterpart of
Lemma

D.3
, controls the behavior of the perturbed weights
w_{m,l}
in their coordinates and alignment with
\theta^{\star}
. Additionally, these weights are approximately orthogonal to each other, which allows for a good characterization of the second moment of the gradient.

One may notice that the definition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}
differs from the non-sparse case, where we explicitly bound the correlation between different
\xi_{m,l}
. This is the benefit of the sparse structure, as two randomly sampled
k
-sparse supports are naturally of low overlap.

Before delving into the component-wise analysis, we begin with a proposition that will be frequently used to calculate the average contribution of each term in the gradient. This proposition serves as the counterpart of
Lemma

G.4
in the sparse case.

Proposition E.4
.

Suppose that the polarization level
\gamma=o(1)
and the noise (
\xi_{m,l},\phi_{m,l})\sim{\text{Unif}}\big({\mathbb{S}}^{k-1}(\phi_{m,l})\big)\otimes{\text{Unif}}({\mathcal{S}}_{k,m})
. Let
\rho=\langle{\theta},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle
where
\theta
is the polarized direction in
w_{m,l}
. Assume that the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,s-\ind\{s\text{ odd}\}}
holds. Then we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{s}]

\displaystyle\simeq\begin{cases}\gamma\rho\cdot\big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{s-1})^{s-1}&\text{if $s$ odd};\\
(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{s})^{s}&\text{if $s$ even},\end{cases}

(E.13)

where
\delta_{s}=(k^{2}/d)^{1/s}=o(1)
for any
s=O(1)
.

Proof of
Proposition

E.4
.

See
Section

E.5
.
∎

Recall that we define the good event over the signal as
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}={\mathcal{E}}_{0,\infty}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\sharp}\cap\bigcap_{r\in S(s^{\star})}{\mathcal{E}}_{0,r}
, where
S({s^{\star}})=\{s^{\star}-1,s^{\star}-\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ odd}\},2s^{\star},4s^{\star}\}
. This definition facilitates our deferred analysis where we need to control multiple moments of different orders and we collect all the necessary good events in
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
in the first place.

E.2
Properties of the Gradient Step

In this section, we preview some properties regarding the gradient defined in Eq. (
E.7
). The following proposition deals with the first moment.

Proposition E.5

(First-order moment of the gradient)
.

Suppose that
\theta^{\star}
is fixed such that the nice event
{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
holds. Under
Definition

E.2
, we choose
\gamma\leq\epsilon=o(1)
such that
\Pr\big({{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)}^{c}\big)\leq O(k^{-s^{\star}})
and

\displaystyle L=\Omega\Big(\log(d)\cdot\big(k\vee(\epsilon^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot k^{s^{\star}+1})\big)\Big).

(E.14)

Then there exists a
\{\xi_{m,l}\}_{l\in[L]}
-measurable event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,1}
with
\Pr({\mathcal{E}}_{m,1})\geq 1-O(k^{-c_{1}})
for some constant
c_{1}>0
, such that on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}
, it holds for any
j\in[d]
that

\displaystyle\langle{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[{\widebar{g}}_{m}]},{e_{j}}\rangle

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s^{\star}}(y)\cdot{\widehat{\psi}}_{{s^{\star}}-1}(y)]\cdot\sqrt{{s^{\star}}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}}[\langle{{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{{s^{\star}}-1}]\theta_{j}^{\star}+R_{m,j},

(E.15)

where the expectation

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}}[\langle{{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{{s^{\star}}-1}]\simeq\begin{cases}\gamma\rho\cdot\big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{{s^{\star}}-2}\big)^{{s^{\star}}-2}&\text{if ${s^{\star}}$ is even};\\
\big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{{s^{\star}}-1}\big)^{{s^{\star}}-1}&\text{if ${s^{\star}}$ is odd},\end{cases}

(E.16)

and
R_{m,j}
is the remainder that can be bounded by

\displaystyle|R_{m,j}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\big(k^{-1}\vee(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|)\big)\cdot\big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+}\big)^{{s^{\star}}-1}+k^{-{s^{\star}}}\Big)\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|

(E.17)

\displaystyle\qquad+(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{{s^{\star}}}\cdot(\gamma|\theta_{j}|+k^{-1/2}\cdot(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}/2})+k^{-({s^{\star}}+1)}.

(E.18)

Proof of
Proposition

E.5
.

See
Section

E.4.1
.
∎

The statement of this proposition clarifies the leading term explicitly, which enables us to track the leading term in the strong alignment more precisely. To complete this section, we provide a proposition that characterizes the fluctuation of the gradient, serving as the counterpart of
Proposition

D.5
.

Proposition E.6

(Fluctuation of mini-batch gradient)
.

Under the simplified setting
Definition

E.2
where
\psi
follows
4.1
. Additionally, suppose that the sample size

\displaystyle n=\Omega\Big(\big((\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log k)^{s^{\star}-1}+L^{-1}\big)^{-1}\cdot\log(d)^{2C_{p}+2}\Big),

(E.19)

where
C_{p}
is the order of the polynomial tail in
(c)
. Then there exists a
\{(z_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
-measurable event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,2}
with
\Pr({\mathcal{E}}_{m,2})\geq 1-O(d^{-(c+1)}/T)
, such that on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,2}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\big|\langle{{\widebar{g}}_{m}},{e_{j}}\rangle-\langle{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[{\widebar{g}}_{m}]},{e_{j}}\rangle\big|\leq\sqrt{\frac{\big((\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log k)^{s^{\star}-1}+L^{-1}\big)\cdot\log(d)}{n}},

(E.20)

for any
j\in[d]
.

Proof of
Proposition

E.6
.

See
Section

E.4.2
.
∎

With these propositions, we have completed the preparation for the analysis of the gradient step and are ready to move on to the proof of the main theorem.

E.3
Proof of the Main Theorem

Proof of
Theorem

5.1
.

Preparations

We first clarify the final good event that we will use throughout the proof. We first fix
\epsilon=k^{-1/2}\cdot\log k
, then it holds by
Lemma

H.15
that for each
m
and
t
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\Big({{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{(t)}(\epsilon)}^{c}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq Ld\cdot O(\exp\{-ck\}+k^{-\log k/4}).

(E.21)

Since
L
and
d
are at most polynomials in
k
, we see that for sufficiently large
k
, it holds that
\Pr\Big({{\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{(t)}(\epsilon)}^{c}\Big)\leq k^{-s^{\star}}
. Additionally, we see that
\gamma=k^{-1/2}
is fixed and our parameter configuration

\displaystyle n=\Omega\Big((k\log^{3}k)^{s^{\star}}\cdot\log d\Big),\quad L=\Omega(k^{({s^{\star}}+3)/2}\cdot\log(k)^{{s^{\star}}-1})

(E.22)

are clearly compatible with the conditions in
Proposition

E.5
and
Proposition

E.6
. At the
t
-th step, the mini-batch
\{(z_{i}^{(t)},y_{i}^{(t)})\}_{i\in[n]}
,
\theta=\theta_{m}^{(t)}
and the error-free gradient

\displaystyle{\widebar{g}}_{m}^{(t)}=\frac{1}{nL}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\big(\psi(y_{i}^{(t)},\langle{w_{m,l}^{(t)}},{z_{i}^{(t)}}\rangle)\cdot z_{i}^{(t)}-{\widehat{\psi}}_{1}(y_{i}^{(t)})\cdot w_{m,l}^{(t)}\big)

(E.23)

together form an instance of
Definition

E.2
, for which we can find the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,1}^{(t)}
and
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,2}^{(t)}
, both with probability at least
1-O(d^{-c-1}/T)
, such that on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,1}^{(t)}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m,2}^{(t)}
the results in
Proposition

E.5
and
Proposition

E.6
hold.

The gradient we use in the algorithm differs from Eq. (
E.23
) by the error term
\mathrm{err}_{m,l,i}^{(t)}\cdot z_{i}^{(t)}
. To control this difference, we define

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{m,3}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\sup_{i}\|z_{i}^{(t)}\|\leq\sqrt{d}\Big\}.

(E.24)

Given our specification on
\sup_{m,l,i,t}\mathrm{err}_{m,l,i}^{(t)}
, it holds on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,3}^{(t)}
that for any
v\in{\mathbb{S}}^{d-1}
:

\displaystyle\big|\|g_{m}^{(t)}\|_{2}-\|{\widebar{g}}_{m}^{(t)}\|_{2}\big|\vee|\langle{g_{m}^{(t)}},{v}\rangle-\langle{{\widebar{g}}_{m}^{(t)}},{v}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\|g_{m}^{(t)}-{\widebar{g}}_{m}^{(t)}\|_{2}

(E.25)

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{i}\|z_{i}^{(t)}\|\cdot\sup_{l}\|\mathrm{err}_{m,l,i}^{(t)}\|_{2}

(E.26)

\displaystyle\leq d^{-9{s^{\star}}}.

(E.27)

Our final event is fixed to be

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{E}}_{0}\cap\bigcap_{m\in[d]}\bigcap_{t=1}^{T}\Big({\mathcal{E}}_{m}^{(t)}(\epsilon)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m,1}^{(t)}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m,2}^{(t)}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m,3}^{(t)}\Big).

(E.28)

With union bound, we have that
\Pr({\mathcal{E}})\geq 1-O(d^{-c})
for some constant
c>0
.

To avoid confusion, we denote for each
m
that
{\widecheck{g}}_{m}^{(t)}={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{{\theta^{\star}}}}[{\widebar{g}}_{m}^{(t)}]
.
Later we will encounter some data dependent index
{\widehat{m}}
and using
{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}}}^{(t)}
avoids the ambiguity of the expectation. With our choice of
n
and
L
,
Proposition

E.6
guarantees that for any
m,t,j
, it holds that

\displaystyle\big|{\widebar{g}}_{m,j}^{(t)}-{\widecheck{g}}_{m,j}^{(t)}\big|

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-({s^{\star}}-1/2)}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k.

(E.29)

In the sequel, we will drop the superscript
t
whenever there is no ambiguity. We will frequently use
{\widetilde{g}}_{m}^{(t)}=P_{\mathsf{Top}_{k}(g_{m}^{(t)})}(g_{m}^{(t)})
.

Weak alignment

The proof towards the weak alignment in the initial step comprises three parts. In the first place, we will show that the index of the gradient we choose
{\widehat{m}}
guarantees that
|\theta_{{\widehat{m}}}^{\star}|\gtrsim k^{-1/2}
.
Thereby, the corresponding gradient exhibits good alignment towards the signal.
Based on this, we can show that the support we choose
\mathsf{Top}_{k}(g_{{\widehat{m}}})
is of considerable quality by successfully identifying
\phi^{\star\star}=\{j:|\theta_{j}^{\star}|\geq 1/\sqrt{2k}\}
.
Combining these elements, we can show that the gradient
{\widetilde{g}}_{{\widehat{m}}}
is well-aligned with the signal
\theta^{\star}
.

We begin with analyzing the quality of
g_{\widehat{m}}
where
\widehat{m}=\mathop{\mathrm{argmax}}_{m}\|{\widetilde{g}}_{m}\|_{2}
.
With this objective in mind, we first work on deriving a signal-dependent upper bound for
\|{\widetilde{g}}_{{\widehat{m}}}\|
.
Note that
\rho_{m}=\langle{\theta_{m}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle=\theta_{m}^{\star}
, and hence
|\rho_{m}|=|\theta_{m}^{\star}|
, where
\theta_{m}=e_{m}
is the initial weight.
Applying
Proposition

E.5
, we have for any
\phi,|\phi|=k
that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\phi}|{\widecheck{g}}_{\widehat{m},j}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{{\widehat{m}}}^{\star}|)^{2\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\}}\cdot\big(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{\widehat{m}}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+}\big)^{2(s^{\star}-1-\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\})}\cdot\sum_{j\in\phi}{\theta^{\star}_{j}}^{2}

(E.30)

\displaystyle\qquad+\big(k^{-2}\vee(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{{\widehat{m}}}^{\star}|)^{2}\cdot(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{{\widehat{m}}}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{2({s^{\star}}-1)}+k^{-2{s^{\star}}}\big)\sum_{j\in\phi}{\theta_{j}^{\star}}^{2}

(E.31)

\displaystyle\qquad+(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{\widehat{m}}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{2s^{\star}}\cdot k^{-1}\sum_{j\in\phi}\Big(\theta_{j}^{2}+(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}}\Big)+k^{-(2{s^{\star}}+1)}

(E.32)

Note that we have
\|\theta^{\star}\|_{\infty}\lesssim k^{-1/2}\cdot\log k
, it holds that

\displaystyle k^{-2}\vee(k^{-1}\cdot|\theta_{\widehat{m}}^{\star}|^{2})\cdot(k^{-1/2}|\theta^{\star}_{\widehat{m}}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{2({s^{\star}}-1)}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-2{s^{\star}}}\cdot\log(k)^{2{s^{\star}}}.

(E.33)

For any
\phi
such that
|\phi|=k
, we have
\sum_{j\in\phi}\theta_{j}^{2}\leq 1
for any
\theta
such that
\|\theta\|_{0}=k
. Therefore, we can further upper bound this quantity by

\displaystyle\sup_{|\phi|=k}\sum_{j\in\phi}|{\widecheck{g}}_{\widehat{m},j}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{{\widehat{m}}}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{2{s^{\star}}-2}+k^{-2{s^{\star}}}\cdot\log(k)^{2{s^{\star}}}

(E.34)

\displaystyle\qquad+(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{{\widehat{m}}}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{2{s^{\star}}}\cdot k^{-1}

(E.35)

\displaystyle\lesssim(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{{\widehat{m}}}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{2{s^{\star}}-2}

(E.36)

\displaystyle\qquad+k^{-2{s^{\star}}}\cdot\log^{s^{\star}/2}k+k^{-2{s^{\star}}}\cdot\log(k)^{2{s^{\star}}}.

(E.37)

Combining Eq. (
E.37
), Eq. (
E.29
) and Eq. (
E.27
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\sup_{|\phi|=k}\sum_{j\in\phi}|g_{\widehat{m},j}|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim k\cdot\sup_{j}|g_{{\widehat{m}},j}-{\widebar{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|^{2}+k\cdot\sup_{j}|{\widebar{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}-{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|^{2}+\sup_{|\phi|=k}\sum_{j\in\phi}|{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|^{2}

(E.38)

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)}\cdot(|\theta^{\star}_{{\widehat{m}}}|+k^{-1/2}\delta_{+})^{2{s^{\star}}-2}+k^{-2{s^{\star}}}\cdot\log(k)^{2{s^{\star}}}.

(E.39)

By definition of
{\widetilde{g}}_{m}
, we can further conclude that

\displaystyle\|{\widetilde{g}}_{\widehat{m}}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\sup_{|\phi|=k}\sum_{j\in\phi}|\langle{g_{\widehat{m}}},{e_{j}}\rangle|^{2}

(E.40)

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-(s^{\star}-1)}\cdot|\theta_{\widehat{m}}^{\star}|^{2(s^{\star}-1)}+o\big(k^{-2(s^{\star}-1)}\big).

(E.41)

On the other hand, for
m\in\phi^{\star\star}
, we have
(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{r}\gtrsim k^{-r}
. Then it holds by
Proposition

E.5
that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\phi^{\star}}|{\widecheck{g}}_{m,j}|^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim k^{-2(s^{\star}-1)}-\widetilde{O}(k^{-2s^{\star}}),

(E.42)

and by Eq. (
E.29
) and (
E.27
), we have that

\displaystyle\|{\widetilde{g}}_{m}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\gtrsim k^{-2(s^{\star}-1)}-\widetilde{O}(k^{-2s^{\star}}).

(E.43)

Now, combining Eq. (
E.43
) with Eq. (
E.41
) by the definition of
{\widehat{m}}
, we have that

\displaystyle k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)}|\theta_{\widehat{m}}^{\star}|^{2(s^{\star}-1)}+o\big(k^{-2(s^{\star}-1)}\big)\gtrsim k^{-2(s^{\star}-1)}-\widetilde{O}(k^{-2s^{\star}}).

(E.44)

We conclude the first step from the last inequality that there exists a global constant
c_{1}>0
such that for sufficiently large
k
:

\displaystyle|\theta_{\widehat{m}}^{\star}|\geq\Big(c_{1}\cdot\big(1-o(1)\big)\cdot k^{-2(s^{\star}-1)}\cdot k^{s^{\star}-1}\Big)^{1/(2(s^{\star}-1))}\geq c_{1}^{\prime}k^{-1/2}.

(E.45)

Now we move on to the support identification. We have shown that
|\theta^{\star}_{\widehat{m}}|\gtrsim k^{-1/2}
. To establish
\phi^{\star\star}\subset\widehat{\phi}=\mathsf{Top}_{k}(g_{\widehat{m}})
, it is sufficient to demonstrate that

\displaystyle\sup_{j\notin\phi^{\star}}|g_{{\widehat{m}},j}|\leq\inf_{j\in\phi^{\star}}|g_{{\widehat{m}},j}|.

(E.46)

In the following, we will bound each side separately. Consider
j\notin\phi^{\star}
, we have by
Proposition

E.5
that

\displaystyle|{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{{\widehat{m}}}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+}\big)^{{s^{\star}}}\cdot\big(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{j}|+k^{-1/2}(k/d)^{1/2}\big)+k^{-({s^{\star}}+1)}.

(E.47)

Combining this upper bound with Eq. (
E.45
) and Eq. (
E.29
) gives us that

\displaystyle|g_{{\widehat{m}},j}|

\displaystyle\leq|g_{{\widehat{m}},j}-{\widebar{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|+|{\widebar{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}-{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|+|{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|

(E.48)

\displaystyle\lesssim(k^{-1/2}\cdot|\theta_{{\widehat{m}}}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{{s^{\star}}}\cdot\big(k^{-1/2}|\theta_{j}|+k^{-1/2}(k/d)^{1/2}\big)+k^{-({s^{\star}}+1)}

(E.49)

\displaystyle\qquad+d^{-9{s^{\star}}}+k^{-({s^{\star}}-1/2)}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k

(E.50)

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-({s^{\star}}-1/2)}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k+\widetilde{O}(k^{-({s^{\star}}+1)}).

(E.51)

On the other hand, for
j\in\phi^{\star\star}
, we have that

\displaystyle|{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|

\displaystyle\gtrsim|k^{-1/2}\theta_{{\widehat{m}}}^{\star}+k^{-1}\delta_{+}|^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|

(E.52)

\displaystyle\gtrsim k^{-({s^{\star}}-1/2)}

(E.53)

Similarly, it holds that

\displaystyle|g_{{\widehat{m}},j}|

\displaystyle\gtrsim|{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|-|g_{{\widehat{m}},j}-{\widebar{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|-|{\widebar{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}-{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|

(E.54)

\displaystyle\gtrsim k^{-({s^{\star}}-1/2)}-o(k^{-({s^{\star}}-1/2)}).

(E.55)

Comparing Eq. (
E.51
) and Eq. (
E.55
), we successfully validate Eq. (
E.46
), and consequently
\phi^{\star\star}\subset\widehat{\phi}
.

We complete our proof of weak alignment by analyzing the inner product between
{\widetilde{g}}_{{\widehat{m}}}
and
\theta^{\star}
and the norm of
{\widetilde{g}}_{{\widehat{m}}}
respectively.
Since
|{\theta^{\star}}_{\widehat{m}}|\gtrsim k^{-1/2}
, we have that

\displaystyle(\gamma|\rho_{\widehat{m}}|+k^{-1/2}\cdot|{\theta^{\star}}_{\widehat{m}}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{s^{\star}-1}\wedge(\gamma|\rho_{\widehat{m}}|+k^{-1/2}\cdot|{\theta^{\star}}_{\widehat{m}}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{s^{\star}-2}\cdot|\gamma\rho_{\widehat{m}}|\gtrsim k^{-(s^{\star}-1)/2}\cdot|\theta^{\star}_{\widehat{m}}|^{{s^{\star}}-1}

(E.56)

For the inner product, we apply
Proposition

E.5
for
{\widehat{m}}
and each
j\in\phi^{\star\star}
and get that

\displaystyle g_{{\widehat{m}},j}\cdot\theta_{j}^{\star}

\displaystyle\simeq{{\theta^{\star}_{j}}^{2}}\cdot k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}\cdot|\theta^{\star}_{{\widehat{m}}}|^{{s^{\star}}-1}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}({\theta^{\star}_{\widehat{m}}})

(E.57)

\displaystyle\qquad-\big(|R_{{\widehat{m}},j}|+|g_{{\widehat{m}},j}-{\widebar{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|+|{\widebar{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}-{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|\big)\cdot|{\theta^{\star}_{j}}|.

(E.58)

Since
\phi^{\star\star}\subset\widehat{\phi}
, we can lower bound the summation of the leading term as

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}}{\theta_{j}^{\star}}^{2}\cdot k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}\cdot|\theta^{\star}_{\widehat{m}}|^{{s^{\star}}-1}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\sum_{j\in\phi^{\star\star}}{\theta_{j}^{\star}}^{2}\cdot k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}\cdot|\theta^{\star}_{\widehat{m}}|^{{s^{\star}}-1}

(E.59)

\displaystyle\gtrsim k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}\cdot|\theta^{\star}_{\widehat{m}}|^{{s^{\star}}-1}\cdot k^{-1}\cdot|\phi^{\star\star}|

(E.60)

\displaystyle\geq k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}\cdot|\theta^{\star}_{\widehat{m}}|^{{s^{\star}}-1},

(E.61)

where the last line holds by the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\sharp}\subset{\mathcal{E}}_{0}
. For the term associated with
R_{{\widehat{m}},j}
, we have by the characterization in
Proposition

E.5
that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}}|R_{{\widehat{m}},j}|\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|

\displaystyle\lesssim(k^{-{s^{\star}}}\cdot\log(k)^{{s^{\star}}}+k^{-{s^{\star}}})\cdot\sum_{j\in\phi^{\star}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{2}

(E.62)

\displaystyle\qquad+k^{-{s^{\star}}}\cdot\log(k)^{{s^{\star}}}\cdot k^{-1/2}\cdot\Big(\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}}|\theta_{j}|\cdot|\theta^{\star}_{j}|+\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}}|\theta^{\star}_{j}|\cdot(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}}\Big)

(E.63)

\displaystyle\qquad+k^{-({s^{\star}}+1)}\cdot\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|.

(E.64)

Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|\cdot\big(|\theta_{j}|+(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq(\sum_{j\in[d]}|\theta^{\star}_{j}|^{2})^{1/2}\cdot\Big((\sum_{j\in[d]}|\theta_{j}|^{2})^{1/2}+(1+k^{3}/d^{2})^{1/2}\Big)

(E.65)

\displaystyle=O(1).

(E.66)

And therefore

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}}|R_{{\widehat{m}},j}|\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-{s^{\star}}}\cdot\log(k)^{{s^{\star}}}+k^{-{s^{\star}}}+\widetilde{O}(k^{-({s^{\star}}+1/2)}).

(E.67)

For the rest of the error terms, we have by Eq. (
E.29
) and Eq. (
E.27
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}}\big(|g_{{\widehat{m}},j}-{\widebar{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|+|{\widebar{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}-{\widecheck{g}}_{{\widehat{m}},j}|\big)\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\in\phi^{\star}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|\cdot k^{-({s^{\star}}-1/2)}\cdot\log(k)^{-3/2}

(E.68)

\displaystyle\leq k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)}\cdot\log(k)^{-3/2}.

(E.69)

Combining Eq. (
E.58
), Eq. (
E.61
), Eq. (
E.67
) and Eq. (
E.69
), we have that

\displaystyle|\langle{{\widetilde{g}}_{\widehat{m}}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|\gtrsim k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}\cdot|\theta^{\star}_{\widehat{m}}|^{{s^{\star}}-1}-o(k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)}).

(E.70)

For the norm, we already have in Eq. (
E.41
) that

\displaystyle\|{\widetilde{g}}_{{\widehat{m}}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}\cdot|\theta_{\widehat{m}}^{\star}|^{({s^{\star}}-1)}+o(k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)}).

(E.71)

Combining the last two inequalities, we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{|\langle{P_{\widehat{\phi}}g_{{\widehat{m}}}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|}{\|P_{\widehat{\phi}}g_{{\widehat{m}}}\|}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{|\theta_{\widehat{m}}^{\star}|^{s^{\star}-1}-o(k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2})}{|\theta_{\widehat{m}}^{\star}|^{s^{\star}-1}+o(k^{-(s^{\star}-1)/2})}=\Theta(1),

(E.72)

given that
|\theta^{\star}_{\widehat{m}}|\geq c^{\prime}_{1}k^{-1/2}
for some constant
c^{\prime}_{1}>0
. This concludes the proof of weak alignment.

Strong Alignment

Starting from the second step, we have by
Algorithm

2
that all the neurons share the same weight parameter.
Let
\theta
be the weight parameter in any step after the first step and we suppose that
\rho=\langle{\theta},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle=\Theta(1)
.
From
Proposition

E.5
, we see that now the choice of the gradient should not change the quality of the gradient significantly, as
\gamma|\rho|\gg k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|
for any
m\in[d]
.
Therefore, we start by analyzing the alignment increment using any
g_{m}
.
This alteration does not affect the alignment increment, but substantially simplifies the analysis.

We additionally define a support
\phi^{\dagger}=\{j\in[d]:|\theta_{j}^{\star}|\geq k^{-1}\}
.
In the following, we fix an arbitrary
m\in[d]
.
We begin with analyzing the magnitude of
g_{m,j}
for
j\in\phi^{\dagger}\setminus\{m\}
.
Since
|\rho|=\Omega(1)
, it holds that

\displaystyle(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}\cdot|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{s^{\star}-1})^{{s^{\star}}-1}\simeq(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}\cdot|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{s^{\star}-2})^{{s^{\star}}-2}\cdot(\gamma|\rho|)\simeq k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}.

(E.73)

With triangle inequality,
Proposition

E.5
indicates that, for
j\in\phi^{\dagger}
:

\displaystyle|g_{m,j}|

\displaystyle\geq|{\widecheck{g}}_{m,j}|-|{\widebar{g}}_{m,j}-{\widecheck{g}}_{m,j}|-|{\widebar{g}}_{m,j}-g_{m,j}|

(E.74)

\displaystyle\geq k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|-k^{-{s^{\star}}/2}\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|

(E.75)

\displaystyle\qquad-k^{-({s^{\star}}+1)/2}\cdot(|\theta_{j}|+(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}/2})-\widetilde{O}(k^{-({s^{\star}}-1/2)}).

(E.76)

Similarly, we have for
j\notin\phi^{\star}
that

\displaystyle|g_{m,j}|

\displaystyle\leq|{\widecheck{g}}_{m,j}|+|{\widebar{g}}_{m,j}-{\widecheck{g}}_{m,j}|+|{\widebar{g}}_{m,j}-g_{m,j}|

(E.77)

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-({s^{\star}}+1)/2}\cdot(|\theta_{j}|+(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}/2})+\widetilde{O}(k^{-({s^{\star}}-1/2)}).

(E.78)

Comparing last two inequalities, we have that
|\theta_{j}^{\star}|>k^{-1}
implies that
|g_{m,j}|\geq\max_{j\notin\phi^{\star}}|g_{m,j}|
for sufficiently large
k
, and therefore

\displaystyle\min_{j\in\phi^{\dagger}}|g_{m,j}|>\max_{j\notin\phi^{\star}}|g_{m,j}|,

(E.79)

which means that
\phi^{\dagger}\subset\widehat{\phi}_{m}=\mathsf{Top}_{k}(g_{m})
. Thereby, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{2}\geq 1-\sum_{j\notin\phi^{\dagger}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{2}\geq 1-k^{-1}.

(E.80)

We then move on to the alignment analysis. Returning to Eq. (
E.108
), we define

\displaystyle\beta_{m}(\rho,\{\xi_{m,l}\}_{l\in[L]},\theta^{\star},\theta)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{{s^{\star}}}(y)\cdot{\widehat{\psi}}_{{s^{\star}}-1}(y)]\cdot\sqrt{{s^{\star}}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{{s^{\star}}-1}]}{\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\rho)^{\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\}}\cdot(\gamma|\rho|)^{s^{\star}-1}};

(E.81)

\displaystyle r_{m,j}(\rho,\{\xi_{m,l}\}_{l\in[L]},\theta^{\star},\theta)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\langle{{\widebar{g}}_{m}},{e_{j}}\rangle]-\theta_{j}^{\star}\cdot(\gamma|\rho|)^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\mathop{\mathrm{sign}}(\rho)^{\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\}}\cdot\beta_{m}.

(E.82)

Then it follows from
Proposition

E.5
that whenever
|\rho|=\Omega(1)
, we have that
\beta_{m}>0
and

\displaystyle\beta_{m}\vee\beta_{m}^{-1}

\displaystyle<B

(E.83)

\displaystyle|r_{m,j}|

\displaystyle\leq r_{m,j}^{a}+r_{m,j}^{b},

(E.84)

where

\displaystyle r_{m,j}^{a}

\displaystyle\leq C_{a}k^{-(s^{\star}+1)/2}\cdot\big(|\theta_{j}|+(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}/2}\big);

(E.85)

\displaystyle r_{m,j}^{b}

\displaystyle\leq C_{b}\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|\cdot(\gamma|\rho|)^{s^{\star}}

(E.86)

with some global positive constant
B,C_{a}
and
C_{0}
, whenever the designated parameters are compatible with the definition of our nice event.
With this representation, we can deduce by Eq. (
E.29
) and Eq. (
E.27
) that

\displaystyle|\langle{{\widetilde{g}}_{m}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|

\displaystyle=\Big|\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}\langle{{\widecheck{g}}_{m}},{e_{j}}\rangle\cdot\theta_{j}^{\star}\Big|+\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}\big(|\langle{g_{m}},{e_{j}}\rangle-\langle{{\widebar{g}}_{m}},{e_{j}}\rangle|+|\langle{{\widebar{g}}_{m}},{e_{j}}\rangle-\langle{{\widecheck{g}}_{m}},{e_{j}}\rangle|\big)\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|

(E.87)

\displaystyle\geq\Big|\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}\langle{{\widecheck{g}}_{m}},{e_{j}}\rangle\cdot\theta_{j}^{\star}\Big|-\sum_{j\in\phi^{\star}}|\theta^{\star}_{j}|\cdot O(k^{-(s^{\star}-1/2)}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k)

(E.89)

\displaystyle\geq\beta_{m}\cdot(\gamma|\rho|)^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{2}-\sum_{j\in\phi^{\star}}|r_{m,j}|\cdot|\theta^{\star}_{j}|-O(k^{-(s^{\star}-1)}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k)

(E.90)

\displaystyle\geq\big(\beta_{m}\cdot(\gamma|\rho|)^{s^{\star}-1}-C_{b}\cdot(\gamma|\rho|)^{{s^{\star}}}\big)\cdot\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{2}-2C_{a}\cdot k^{-(s^{\star}+1)/2}-O(k^{-(s^{\star}-1)}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k).

(E.91)

where the last inequality holds by Eq. (
E.66
).
To complete the analysis, we need an upper bound for
\|{\widetilde{g}}_{m}\|_{2}
. Note that by the triangle inequality and Eq. (
E.82
), we have that

\displaystyle\|{\widetilde{g}}_{m}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|P_{\widehat{\phi}_{m}}{\widecheck{g}}_{m}\|+\|P_{\widehat{\phi}_{m}}{\widebar{g}}_{m}-P_{\widehat{\phi}_{m}}{\widecheck{g}}_{m}\|+\|P_{\widehat{\phi}_{m}}{\widebar{g}}_{m}-P_{\widehat{\phi}_{m}}g_{m}\|

(E.92)

\displaystyle\leq\|\beta_{m}(\gamma|\rho|)^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot P_{\widehat{\phi}_{m}}\theta^{\star}\|+\|P_{\widehat{\phi}_{m}}r_{m}^{a}\|+\|P_{\widehat{\phi}_{m}}r_{m}^{b}\|

(E.93)

\displaystyle\qquad+O(k^{-(s^{\star}-1)}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k),

(E.94)

where
r_{m}^{a}=(r_{m,1},\dots,r_{m,d}^{a})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
r_{m}^{b}=(r_{m,1},\dots,r_{m,d}^{b})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
. To proceed, note that by Eq. (
E.86
) and Eq. (
E.29
), we have that

\displaystyle\|P_{\widehat{\phi}}r_{m}^{a}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{a}k^{-(s^{\star}+1)/2}\cdot(\|\theta\|_{2}+\sqrt{1+k^{2}/d})

(E.95)

\displaystyle\leq 3C_{a}k^{-(s^{\star}+1)/2};

(E.96)

\displaystyle\|P_{\widehat{\phi}}r_{m}^{b}\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{b}\cdot(\gamma|\rho|)^{s^{\star}}\cdot\Big({\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}|\theta^{\star}_{j}|^{2}}\Big)^{1/2}.

(E.97)

Putting these upper bounds together, we have that

\displaystyle\|P_{\widehat{\phi}}g_{m}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big(\beta_{m}\cdot(\gamma|\rho|)^{s^{\star}-1}+C_{b}(\gamma|\rho|)^{s^{\star}}\big)\cdot\Big({\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}|\theta^{\star}_{j}|^{2}}\Big)^{1/2}

(E.98)

\displaystyle\qquad+3C_{a}k^{-(s^{\star}+1)/2}+O(k^{-(s^{\star}-1)}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k).

(E.99)

Note that for any
a_{1}\wedge a_{2}>b>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{a_{1}-b}{a_{2}+b}=

\displaystyle\frac{(a_{1}-b)\cdot(a_{2}-b)}{a_{2}^{2}-b^{2}}\geq(a_{1}/a_{2}-b/a_{2})\cdot(1-b/a_{2}).

(E.100)

Setting
\Delta=k^{-1}\vee\big(k^{-(s^{\star}-1)/2}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k\big)=o(1)
, we get by combining Eq. (
E.91
) and Eq. (
E.99
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{{\widetilde{g}}_{m}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle}{\|{\widetilde{g}}_{m}\|}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{(\beta_{m}-C_{b}\cdot\gamma|\rho|)\cdot\Big(\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}{\theta_{j}^{\star}}^{2}\Big)-3C_{a}\cdot k^{-1}-O(k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k)}{\big(\beta_{m}+C_{b}\cdot\gamma|\rho|\big)\cdot\Big(\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}{\theta_{j}^{\star}}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+3C_{a}\cdot k^{-1}+O(k^{-({s^{\star}}-1)/2}\cdot\log^{-3/2}k)}

(E.101)

\displaystyle\geq\big(1-O(\Delta)\big)^{-1}\cdot\Big(\frac{1-C_{b}\beta_{m}^{-1}\gamma|\rho|}{1+C_{b}\beta_{m}^{-1}\gamma|\rho|}\cdot(\sum_{j\in\widehat{\phi}_{m}}{\theta_{j}^{\star}}^{2})^{1/2}-O(\Delta)\Big)

(E.102)

\displaystyle\geq 1-C\cdot k^{-1}-O(\Delta)

(E.103)

where the last line holds because
(1-Ck^{-1})^{r}\geq 1-rC\cdot k^{-1}
for any
C,r>0
and sufficiently large
k
. Note that
k^{-1}=O(\Delta)
, we see that

\displaystyle\frac{\langle{{\widetilde{g}}_{m}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle}{\|{\widetilde{g}}_{m}\|}

\displaystyle\geq 1-O(\Delta).

(E.104)

Since
Algorithm

2
updates all neurons by gradient sharing in Line
8
, the same normalized vector is assigned to every
\theta_{m}^{(T)}
. Therefore the above alignment bound holds for all
m\in[M]
, concluding the proof of
Theorem

5.1
.

∎

E.4
Proof of the Key Results

E.4.1
Proof of
Proposition

E.5

Proof of
Proposition

E.5
.

First, by
Lemma

G.2
, we have for each
j\in[d]
that

\displaystyle\langle{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[{\widebar{g}}_{m}]},{e_{j}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\geq{s^{\star}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)\cdot{\widehat{\psi}}_{s+1}(y)]\cdot\frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle^{s}\cdot\langle{w_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle

(E.105)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{s\geq{s^{\star}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)\cdot{\widehat{\psi}}_{s-1}(y)]\cdot\frac{\sqrt{s}}{L}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{L}\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle^{s-1}\cdot\langle{{\theta^{\star}}},{e_{j}}\rangle.

(E.106)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{{s^{\star}}}(y)\cdot{\widehat{\psi}}_{{s^{\star}}-1}(y)]\cdot\frac{\sqrt{{s^{\star}}}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle^{{s^{\star}}-1}\cdot\langle{{\theta^{\star}}},{e_{j}}\rangle

(E.107)

\displaystyle\qquad+R_{1}+R_{2},

(E.108)

where the remainders
R_{1},R_{2}
are defined as

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\geq{s^{\star}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s+1}(y)\cdot{\widehat{\psi}}_{s}(y)]\cdot\frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle^{s}\cdot\langle{{\theta^{\star}}},{e_{j}}\rangle;

(E.109)

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\geq{s^{\star}}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)\cdot{\widehat{\psi}}_{s+1}(y)]\cdot\frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L}\cdot\sum_{l=1}^{L}\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle^{s}\cdot\langle{w_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle.

(E.110)

We also denote the leading signal term as

\displaystyle S={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{{s^{\star}}}(y)\cdot{\widehat{\psi}}_{{s^{\star}}-1}(y)]\cdot\frac{\sqrt{{s^{\star}}}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle^{{s^{\star}}-1}\cdot\langle{{\theta^{\star}}},{e_{j}}\rangle.

(E.111)

By definition
R_{1}
collects the higher order term that aligns with the signal and
R_{2}
collects all the terms in the expected gradient that are parallel to
w_{m,l}
. In comparison to the non-sparse case, here we are analyzing the gradient coordinate-wisely. Therefore,
R_{1}
and
R_{2}
need to be controlled separately.

Analysis for the dominant term
S
in Eq. (
E.108
)

We first define

\displaystyle{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}=w_{m,l}\cdot\ind\{\sup_{j}|\langle{\xi_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|\leq\epsilon\}.

(E.112)

By definition of
\widetilde{w}_{m,l}
, we have that
\widetilde{w}_{m,l},l\in[L]
are independent of each other and
{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}=w_{m,l}
on event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
. We can approximate the expectation of
\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle
as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}}[\langle{{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle^{s^{\star}-1}]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}\big[\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\ind\big\{\sup_{j}|\langle{\xi_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|\leq\epsilon\big\}\big]

(E.113)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle^{s^{\star}-1}]\pm\Pr\big(\sup_{j}|\langle{\xi_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|>\epsilon\big)

(E.114)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle^{s^{\star}-1}]\pm\Pr({{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)}^{c}).

(E.115)

Here the last line holds because
{{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)}^{c}=\cup_{l}\{\sup_{j}|\langle{\xi_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|>\epsilon\}
.
For the first term, by
Proposition

E.4
, we have that on
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,s^{\star}-1-\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\}}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{s^{\star}-1}]

\displaystyle\simeq\begin{cases}\gamma\rho\cdot\big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{s^{\star}-2}\big)^{s^{\star}-2}&\text{if $s^{\star}$ even};\\
(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{s^{\star}-1})^{s^{\star}-1}&\text{if $s^{\star}$ odd}.\end{cases}

(E.116)

For the second moment that is involved in the Bernstein’s inequality, we have that on
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,2s^{\star}-2}

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\langle{\widetilde{w}_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{2s^{\star}-2}]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{2s^{\star}-2}\ind\{\sup_{j}|\langle{\xi_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|\leq\epsilon\}]

(E.117)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{2s^{\star}-2}]

(E.118)

\displaystyle\simeq(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{2{s^{\star}}-2})^{2s^{\star}-2}

(E.119)

To proceed, we have by Bernstein’s inequality (
Lemma

H.10
) that there exists an event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,11}
with
\Pr({\mathcal{E}}_{m,11})\geq 1-O(d^{-c_{b,11}})
. It holds on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,11}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l}\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{s^{\star}-1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l}\langle{{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{s^{\star}-1}

(E.120)

\displaystyle\simeq{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{s^{\star}-1}]+E,

(E.121)

where the error term
E
can be bounded by

\displaystyle|E|

\displaystyle\leq\big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{2{s^{\star}}-2}\big)^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{L}}+\frac{\epsilon^{s^{\star}-1}\log(d)}{L}+\Pr\big({{\mathcal{E}}}^{c}_{m}(\epsilon)\big).

(E.122)

Moreover, the assumption that

\displaystyle L

\displaystyle\gtrsim\log d\cdot\Big(k^{2}\vee\Big(\epsilon^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot k^{s^{\star}}\Big)\Big);\qquad\Pr({{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)}^{c})\leq k^{-s^{\star}};

(E.123)

allows us to simplify the upper bound for
E
, since

\displaystyle|E|

\displaystyle\lesssim k^{-1}\cdot\big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{2{s^{\star}}-2}\big)^{s^{\star}-1}+k^{-s^{\star}}.

(E.124)

In conclusion, we have on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,11}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
that

\displaystyle S\simeq({\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{s^{\star}-1}]+E)\cdot\langle{\theta^{\star}},{e_{j}}\rangle.

(E.125)

We retain this form for further simplification.

Analysis for the first remainder
R_{1}
in Eq. (
E.108
)

For any
s,s^{\prime}
, it holds by the property of likelihood ratio decomposition that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[|\zeta_{s}(y)\cdot{\widehat{\psi}}_{s^{\prime}}(y)|]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\zeta_{s}(y)^{2}]^{1/2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[{\widehat{\psi}}_{s^{\prime}}(y)^{2}]^{1/2}

(E.126)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{s^{\prime}\geq 0}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[{\widehat{\psi}}_{s^{\prime}}(y)^{2}]},

(E.127)

and the last quantity is a constant that is independent of
s,s^{\prime}
. To bound the summation for
s\geq{s^{\star}}
, we use
Lemma

E.3
, which gives
|\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle|\leq\gamma|\rho|+\epsilon<1/2
on the nice event. Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{s\geq{s^{\star}}}\frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle|^{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{s\geq{s^{\star}}}\sqrt{s+1}\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{2}\Big)^{s-{s^{\star}}}\cdot\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle|^{{s^{\star}}}

(E.128)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle|^{{s^{\star}}}.

(E.129)

Now it reduces to bounding the right-hand side of Eq. (
E.129
).
Note that on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
,
{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}=w_{m,l}
. We can first track the first and second moment of
\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle
as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\widetilde{w}_{m,l}}[|\langle{\widetilde{w}_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|^{s^{\star}}]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[|\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|^{s^{\star}}]

(E.130)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{2s^{\star}}]^{1/2}.

(E.131)

To bound the last quantity, we see that given
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,2s^{\star}}
,
Proposition

E.4
reads

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{2s^{\star}}]

\displaystyle\leq(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{2s^{\star}}.

(E.132)

By Bernstein’s inequality, there exists an event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,12}
with
\Pr({\mathcal{E}}_{m,12})\geq 1-O(d^{-c_{b,12}})
such that on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,12}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
, it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|^{s^{\star}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(1+\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{L}}\Big)\cdot(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{s^{\star}}+\frac{\epsilon^{s^{\star}}\log(d)}{L},

(E.133)

Given that
L\gtrsim\log(d)\cdot\big(k\vee(\epsilon^{s^{\star}}\cdot k^{s^{\star}})\big)
, it further holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|^{s^{\star}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{s^{\star}}+k^{-s^{\star}}.

(E.134)

In conclusion, it holds on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,12}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
that

\displaystyle R_{1}\lesssim\Big((\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{s^{\star}-1}+k^{-s^{\star}}\Big)\cdot|\langle{\theta^{\star}},{e_{j}}\rangle|.

(E.135)

Analysis for the second remainder
R_{2}
in Eq. (
E.108
)

Similar to Eq. (
E.129
), we can first upper bound
R_{2}
as

\displaystyle|R_{2}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|^{s^{\star}}\cdot|\langle{w_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|

(E.136)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}|\langle{{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|^{s^{\star}}\cdot|\langle{{\widetilde{w}}_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|,

(E.137)

where the last line holds by the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
.
We decouple the product with the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\widetilde{w}_{m,l}}[|\langle{\widetilde{w}_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|^{s^{\star}}\cdot|\langle{\widetilde{w}_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[|\langle{w_{m,l}},{{\theta^{\star}}}\rangle|^{s^{\star}}\cdot|\langle{w_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|]

(E.138)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[|\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|^{2s^{\star}}]^{1/2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[|\langle{w_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|^{2}]^{1/2}

(E.139)

The first term in the upper bound can be tackled with
Proposition

E.4
. For the second term, we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{w_{m,l}}[\langle{w_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}[(\langle{\xi_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle+\gamma\cdot\langle{\theta},{e_{j}}\rangle)^{2}]

(E.140)

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}[\langle{\xi_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle^{2}]+\gamma^{2}\theta_{j}^{2}

(E.141)

\displaystyle=\gamma^{2}\theta_{j}^{2}+{\mathbb{E}}[\ind\{j\in\phi_{m,l}\}\cdot\xi_{m,l,j}^{2}]

(E.142)

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{2}\theta_{j}^{2}+k^{-1}\cdot(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}}.

(E.143)

Here, the first line holds because
\|\gamma\theta+\xi\|_{2}\geq 1/2
. The last line holds by applying
Lemma

H.5
and that
{\mathbb{P}}(j\in\phi_{m,l})\leq k/d
for
j\neq m
. Note that each term in the summation is bounded by
\epsilon\log(k)
up to a constant on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
. By Bernstein’s inequality (
Lemma

H.10
), there exists an event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,13}
with
\Pr({\mathcal{E}}_{m,13})\geq 1-O(d^{-c_{b,13}})
. It holds on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,13}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
that

\displaystyle|R_{2}|{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\Bigg(1+\sqrt{\frac{\log(d)}{L}}\Bigg)\cdot\Big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+}\Big)^{s^{\star}}\cdot\big(\gamma|\theta_{j}|+k^{-1/2}(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}/2}\big)

(E.144)

\displaystyle+\frac{\epsilon^{s^{\star}+1}\log(d)}{L}.

(E.145)

Given that

\displaystyle L\gtrsim\log(d)\cdot\Big(k\vee(\epsilon^{s^{\star}+1}\cdot k^{s^{\star}+1})\Big),

(E.146)

we conclude that it holds on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,13}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
that

\displaystyle|R_{2}|{\lesssim}

\displaystyle\Big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+}\Big)^{s^{\star}}\cdot\big(\gamma|\theta_{j}|+k^{-1/2}(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}/2}\big)+k^{-(s^{\star}+1)}.

(E.147)

Summary of first-order moment

We now merge previous results to summarize the results for the first-order moment.
Note that it is sufficient to set

\displaystyle L=\Omega\Big(\log(d)\cdot\big(k\vee\epsilon^{s^{\star}-1}(k\cdot\log k)^{s^{\star}+1}\big)\Big)

(E.148)

Define the final event as
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,1}={\mathcal{E}}_{m,11}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m,12}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m,13}
, which is
\{w_{m,l}\}_{l\in[L]}
measurable.
By previous analysis, it holds on this event that

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle\simeq\theta_{j}^{\star}\cdot(\gamma\rho)^{\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\}}\cdot\big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{s^{\star}-1-\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\}}\big)^{s^{\star}-1-\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\}}+\theta_{j}^{\star}\cdot E;

(E.149)

\displaystyle R_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+}\big)^{s^{\star}}+k^{-s^{\star}}\Big)\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|;

(E.150)

\displaystyle R_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+}\Big)^{s^{\star}}\cdot(\gamma|\theta_{j}|+k^{-1/2}\cdot(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}/2})+k^{-({s^{\star}}+1)}.

(E.151)

Following the error term
E
in Eq. (
E.124
), we define
R=R_{1}+R_{2}+E
, which can be bounded by

\displaystyle|R|

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(k^{-1}\vee(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|)\cdot(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+})^{s^{\star}-1}+k^{-s^{\star}}\Big)\cdot|\theta_{j}^{\star}|

(E.152)

\displaystyle\qquad+\Big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{+}\Big)^{s^{\star}}\cdot(\gamma|\theta_{j}|+k^{-1/2}\cdot(k/d)^{\ind\{j\neq m\}/2})+k^{-(s^{\star}+1)}.

(E.153)

And we summarize the first moment on
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,1}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\langle{{\widebar{g}}_{m}},{e_{j}}\rangle]

\displaystyle\simeq\theta_{j}^{\star}\cdot(\gamma\rho)^{\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\}}\cdot\big(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{s^{\star}-1-\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\}}\big)^{s^{\star}-1-\ind\{s^{\star}\text{ even}\}}+R,

(E.154)

where
R
is upper bounded in Eq. (
E.153
).
∎

E.4.2
Proof of
Proposition

E.6

Proof of
Proposition

E.6
.

Similar to the proof of
Proposition

D.5
, the proof of this proposition comprises two parts. To begin with, we calculate the variance of each coordinate of
{\widebar{g}}_{m}
.

Second moment calculation

It suffices to consider the variance of the first sample. To this end, we define

\displaystyle{\widebar{g}}_{m,1}=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\big(\psi(y_{1},\langle{w_{m,l}},{z_{1}}\rangle)\cdot z_{1}-{\widehat{\psi}}_{1}(y_{1})\cdot w_{m,l}\big).

(E.155)

For any
v\in\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{d}\}
, it holds by the definition of
{\widebar{g}}_{m,1}
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\langle{\widebar{g}}_{m,1},v\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{l,l^{\prime}=1}^{L}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w_{l},z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w_{l^{\prime}},z\rangle)\langle z,v\rangle^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{l,l^{\prime}=1}^{L}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}\left[\widehat{\psi}_{1}(y)^{2}\langle w_{l},v\rangle\langle w_{l^{\prime}},v\rangle\right]

(E.156)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{l\neq l^{\prime}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\bigg[\psi(y,\langle w_{l},z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w_{l^{\prime}},z\rangle)\langle z,v\rangle^{2}\cdot\bigg(1+\sum_{s\geq s^{\star}}\zeta_{s}(y)h_{s}(\langle\theta^{\star},z\rangle)\bigg)\bigg]

(E.157)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w_{l},z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w_{l},z\rangle)\langle z,v\rangle^{2}\right]+\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{l\neq l^{\prime}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\widehat{\psi}_{1}(y)^{2}]\langle w_{l},v\rangle\langle w_{l^{\prime}},v\rangle

(E.158)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\widehat{\psi}_{1}(y)^{2}]\langle w_{l},v\rangle^{2}.

(E.159)

In the same manner as the non-sparse case, we can derive an
O(1/L)
upper bound for the second and the last summation, which run over all
l=l^{\prime}
.
By
Lemma

E.3
, we already have that

\displaystyle\sup_{l,j}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma+\epsilon\coloneqq\epsilon_{\textsf{chart}};

(E.160)

\displaystyle\sup_{l}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma|\rho|+\epsilon\coloneqq\epsilon_{\textsf{signal}};

(E.161)

\displaystyle\sup_{l\neq l^{\prime}}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{w_{m,l^{\prime}}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log(k)\coloneqq\epsilon_{\textsf{mutual}}.

(E.162)

Applying
Lemma

G.3
, the desired expectation is well behaved if the ratio
{\epsilon_{\textsf{chart}}^{2}\vee\epsilon_{\textsf{signal}}^{2}}/{\epsilon_{\textsf{mutual}}}
is a constant-bounded term. To validate this fact, we note that

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon_{\textsf{chart}}^{2}\vee\epsilon_{\textsf{signal}}^{2}}{\epsilon_{\textsf{mutual}}}\lesssim\frac{\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}}{\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log(k)}.

(E.163)

Since
\epsilon\ll 1\ll\log(k)^{1/2}
, we conclude that
{\epsilon_{\textsf{chart}}^{2}\vee\epsilon_{\textsf{signal}}^{2}}/{\epsilon_{\textsf{mutual}}}\lesssim 1
for sufficiently large
k
. Therefore, we have by
Lemma

G.3
that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{l\neq l^{\prime}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\Big[\psi(y_{1},\langle{w_{m,l}},{z_{1}}\rangle)\cdot\psi(y_{1},\langle{w_{m,l^{\prime}}},{z_{1}}\rangle)\cdot\langle{z_{1}},{v}\rangle^{2}\cdot\Big(1+\sum_{s=s^{\star}}^{\infty}\zeta_{s}(y)h_{s}(\langle{\theta^{\star}},{z}\rangle)\Big)\Big]\lesssim\epsilon_{\textsf{mutual}}^{s^{\star}-1}.

(E.164)

On the other hand, we have for the third term in Eq. (
E.159
) that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L^{2}}\sum_{l\neq l^{\prime}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\widehat{\psi}_{1}(y)^{2}]\langle{w_{m,l}},{v}\rangle\cdot\langle{w_{m,l^{\prime}}},{v}\rangle

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{l}|\langle{w_{m,l^{\prime}}},{v}\rangle|^{2}\cdot\ind\{s^{\star}\leq 2\}

(E.165)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{\textsf{chart}}^{2}\cdot\ind\{s^{\star}\leq 2\}

(E.166)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{\textsf{mutual}}\cdot\ind\{s^{\star}\leq 2\},

(E.167)

where the first line holds by
Lemma

E.3
.

In summary, we have on the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}
that

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{d}\}}n\Var_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\langle{g_{m}},{v}\rangle]

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{2}^{s^{\star}-1}+\frac{1}{L}=\big(\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log(k)\big)^{s^{\star}-1}+\frac{1}{L}.

(E.168)

Concentration

We now turn to validate the condition of
Lemma

H.12
. For any
v\in\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{d}\}
, we set
G(z,y,w)=|\psi(y,\langle{w},{z}\rangle)\cdot\langle{z},{v}\rangle|+|{\widehat{\psi}}_{1}(y)\cdot\langle{w},{v}\rangle|
with the domain measure defined as
\mathrm{d}{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}(z_{1},y_{1})\times\mathrm{d}\mu(w)
, where

d\mu(w)=L^{-1}\sum_{l}\delta_{w_{m,l}}
, the integral Minkowski’s inequality implies that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[|\langle{g_{m,1}},{v}\rangle|^{r}]^{1/r}

\displaystyle=\Big(\int\mathrm{d}{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}(y,z)\Big(\int\mathrm{d}\mu(w)|G(z,y,w)|\Big)^{r}\Big)^{1/r}

(E.169)

\displaystyle\leq\int\mathrm{d}\mu(w)\Big(\int\mathrm{d}{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}(y,z)|G(z,y,w)|^{r}\Big)^{1/r}

(E.170)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[|\psi(y_{i},\langle{w_{m,l}},{z_{i}}\rangle)\cdot\langle{z_{i}},{v}\rangle|^{r}]^{1/r}+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l}|\langle{w_{m,l}},{v}\rangle|.

(E.171)

To proceed, we leverage Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to decouple the average of the product in the first term, which reads

\displaystyle\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[|\psi(y_{i},\langle{w_{m,l}},{z_{i}}\rangle)\cdot\langle{z_{i}},{v}\rangle|^{r}]^{1/r}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[|\psi(y_{i},\langle{w_{m,l}},{z_{i}}\rangle)|^{2r}]^{1/2r}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[|\langle{z_{i}},{v}\rangle|^{2r}]^{1/2r}.

(E.172)

Similar to the proof of
Proposition

D.5
, we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\psi(y,\langle{w_{m,l}},{z}\rangle)^{2r}]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[U_{\langle{{\theta^{\star}}},{w_{m,l}}\rangle}\Big(\frac{{\mathbb{P}}(x,y)}{{\mathbb{Q}}(x,y)}\Big)^{2}]^{1/2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\psi(y,x)^{4r}]^{1/2}\lesssim r^{C_{p}4r},

(E.173)

where the first inequality exactly repeats Eq. (
D.114
) and the second inequality holds by
(c)
. On the other hand, we have that
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\langle{z_{i}},{v}\rangle^{2r}]^{1/2r}\leq r^{1/2}
. Since the second term in Eq. (
E.171
) is bounded by
O(1)
, we conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[|\langle{g_{m,1}},{v}\rangle|^{r}]^{1/r}

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{C_{p}+1/2}.

(E.174)

Thus,
Lemma

H.12
implies that there exists a
\{(z_{i},y_{i})\}_{i\in[n]}
-measurable event
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,2}
with probability at least
1-O(d^{-c-1}/T)
, on which for any
v\in\{e_{1},e_{2},\dots,e_{d}\}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\big|\langle{g_{m}},{v}\rangle-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\langle{g_{m}},{v}\rangle]\big|

\displaystyle{\lesssim}\sqrt{\frac{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\langle{g_{m,1}},{v}\rangle^{2}]\cdot\log(d^{c+1}T)}{n}}+\frac{\log(d^{c+1}T)\cdot\log(d^{c+1}Tn)^{C_{p}+1/2}}{n}

(E.175)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sqrt{\frac{\big(\big(\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log(k)\big)^{s^{\star}-1}+L^{-1}\big)\cdot\log(d)}{n}}+\frac{\log(d)^{C_{p}+3/2}}{n},

(E.176)

given that
T,n
are at most of polynomial rate in
d
. Since we assume that

\displaystyle n=\Omega\Big(\big({(\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log(k))^{s^{\star}-1}+L^{-1}}\big)^{-1}\cdot\log(d)^{2C_{p}+2}\Big),

(E.177)

the above inequality can be further simplified as

\displaystyle\big|\langle{g_{m}},{v}\rangle-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\langle{g_{m}},{v}\rangle]\big|

\displaystyle{\lesssim}\sqrt{\frac{\big(\big(\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log(k)\big)^{s^{\star}-1}+L^{-1}\big)\cdot\log(d)}{n}}.

(E.178)

Additionally,
{\mathcal{E}}_{m,2}
is the desired event. This concludes the proof of
Proposition

E.6
.
∎

E.5
Proofs for Technical Results in the Sparse Case

Proof of
Lemma

E.1
.

With a slight abuse of notation, we assume that
\theta^{\star}\sim{\text{Unif}}({\mathbb{S}}^{k-1})
. We first consider the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\infty}=\{\|\theta^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq C\cdot k^{-1/2}\log(k)^{1/2}\}
. From the proof of
Lemma

H.15
, we see that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\|\theta^{\star}\|_{\infty}\geq t)\leq 2k\cdot{\mathbb{P}}(\theta^{\star}_{1}\geq t)\leq 2k\exp(-k/16)+2k\exp(-t^{2}k/4).

(E.179)

Take
t=C\cdot k^{-1/2}\log(k)^{1/2}
, we have that the failure probability is upper bounded by
2k\exp(-k/16)+2k^{1-C^{2}/4}
.

For the
r
-norm, we leverage the property that
\theta^{\star}\overset{d.}{=}{Z/\|Z\|_{2}}
where
Z\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{k})
. Now,

\|Z\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i\leq k}Z_{i}^{2}
, where
Z_{i}^{2}-{\mathbb{E}}[Z_{i}^{2}]\geq-1
. Applying one-sided Bernstein’s inequality with failure probability
k^{-c_{0}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\|Z\|_{2}^{2}\leq k+\sqrt{2c_{0}k\log(k)}+c_{0}\log(k)/3.

(E.180)

On the other hand, note that we have for
c_{1}>2
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\max_{i\leq k}|Z_{i}|>\sqrt{2c_{1}\log k})\leq k\cdot{\mathbb{P}}(|Z_{1}|>\sqrt{2c_{1}\log k})\leq k\exp\{-c\log k\}=k^{1-c_{1}},

(E.181)

To apply Bernstein’s inequality, we note that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[|Z_{i}|^{r}\cdot\ind\{|Z_{1}|\leq\sqrt{2c_{1}\log k}\}]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}[|Z_{i}|^{2r}]^{1/2};

(E.182)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[|Z_{i}|^{2r}\cdot\ind\{|Z_{1}|\leq\sqrt{2c_{1}\log k}\}]

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}[|Z_{i}|^{2r}],

(E.183)

where
{\mathbb{E}}[Z_{i}^{2r}]=(2r-1)!!
. Therefore, it holds by truncated Bernstein’s inequality that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\|Z\|_{r}^{r}>(k+\sqrt{2c_{2}k\log(k)})\cdot{\mathbb{E}}[|Z_{1}|^{2r}]^{1/2}+\big(\sqrt{2C\log(k)}\big)^{r}\cdot c_{2}\log(k)/3\Big)\leq k^{1-c_{1}}+k^{-c_{2}}

(E.184)

Combining the two bounds, we conclude that with probability
1-O(k^{1-c_{0}\vee c_{1}\vee c_{2}})
, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\theta^{\star}\|_{r}^{r}\overset{d.}{=}\frac{\|Z\|_{r}^{r}}{\|Z\|_{2}^{r}}\lesssim\frac{k+\sqrt{k\log k}+(\log k)^{1+r/2}}{(k+\sqrt{k\log k}+\log k)^{r/2}}\lesssim k^{1-r/2},

(E.185)

with probability at least
1-O(k^{-c})
for some constant
c>0
.

We now move on to consider the event
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,\sharp}=\big\{\sum_{i\leq k}\ind\{|\theta_{i}^{\star}|\geq 1/\sqrt{2k}\}\geq k/4\big\}
.
First, it follows from Hoeffding’s inequality that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\sum_{i\leq k}\ind\{Z_{i}^{2}\geq 3/4\}\geq k/4\Big)

\displaystyle\geq 1-2\exp(-2(p-1/4)^{2}k),

(E.186)

where
p={\mathbb{P}}(Z_{1}^{2}\geq 3/4)>1/4
. Denote the above event as
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}
.
On the other hand, we have by the Bernstein’s inequality that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\Big(\Big|k^{-1}\sum_{i\leq k}Z_{i}^{2}-1\Big|\leq 1/2\Big)\geq 1-2\exp\{-k/32\}.

(E.187)

Denote the above event as
{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
. Then on the event
{\mathcal{A}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{2}
, we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{i\leq k}\ind\Big\{\frac{|Z_{i}|}{\|Z\|}>\frac{1}{\sqrt{2k}}\Big\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\leq k}\ind\Big\{Z_{i}^{2}>\frac{1}{2k}\sum_{i\leq k}Z_{i}^{2}\Big\}

(E.188)

\displaystyle\overset{{\mathcal{A}}_{2}}{\geq}\sum_{i\leq k}\ind\{Z_{i}^{2}>\frac{3}{4}\}

(E.189)

\displaystyle\overset{{\mathcal{A}}_{1}}{\geq}k/4.

(E.190)

In conclusion we have that
{\mathbb{P}}(|\{i:|\theta_{i}^{\star}|>1/\sqrt{2k}\}|>k/4)\geq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{A}}_{1}\cap{\mathcal{A}}_{2})\geq 1-\exp\{-c_{3}k\}
for some constant
c_{3}>0
.

∎

Proof of
Lemma

E.3
.

Clearly, it holds that

\displaystyle\|\gamma\theta+\xi_{m,l}\|_{2}\geq\|\xi_{m,l}\|_{2}-\gamma\cdot\|\xi_{m,l}\|_{2}\geq 1/2.

(E.191)

By substituting this lower bound for the denominator, we have for any
j,l
that

\displaystyle|\langle{w_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq 2(\gamma|\theta_{j}|+|\langle{\xi_{m,l}},{e_{j}}\rangle|)

(E.192)

\displaystyle\leq 2(\gamma|\theta_{j}|+\epsilon).

(E.193)

The last line holds by the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
.
On the other hand, we have for any
l
that

\displaystyle|\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big(\gamma|\rho|+\Big|\sum_{j\in[d]}\xi_{m,l,j}\cdot\theta_{j}^{\star}\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi^{\star}\cap\phi_{m,l}\}\Big|\Big)

(E.194)

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma|\rho|+2\big(\sum_{j}\xi_{m,l,j}^{2}\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi^{\star}\cap\phi_{m,l}\}\big)^{1/2}\cdot\big(\sum_{j}{\theta^{\star}_{j}}^{2}\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi^{\star}\cap\phi_{m,l}\}\big)^{1/2}

(E.195)

\displaystyle\leq 2\gamma|\rho|+2\sup_{j}|\xi_{m,l,j}|\cdot\|\theta^{\star}\|_{\infty}\cdot|\phi^{\star}\cap\phi_{m,l}|

(E.196)

To proceed, note that on the event
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{m}\cap{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
, it holds that
|\phi^{\star}\cap\phi_{m,l}|\leq\log k
and that
\sup_{j}|\xi_{m,l,j}|\leq\epsilon
. Since we assume that
\|\theta^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq 1/\log k
, it holds that

\displaystyle|\langle{w_{m,l}},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle|\leq 2(\gamma|\rho|+\epsilon).

(E.197)

Now we turn to consider the correlation between
w_{m,l}
and
w_{m,l^{\prime}}
.

\displaystyle|\langle{w_{m,l}},{w_{m,l^{\prime}}}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big(\gamma^{2}+\sum_{j}|\xi_{m,l,j}|\cdot|{\xi_{m,l^{\prime},j}}|\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi_{m,l}\cap\phi_{m,l^{\prime}}\}

(E.198)

\displaystyle\qquad+\gamma\sum_{j}|\theta_{j}|\cdot|\xi_{m,l,j}|\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi_{m,l}\cap\mathsf{supp}(\theta)\}

(E.199)

\displaystyle\qquad+\gamma\sum_{j}|\theta_{j}|\cdot|\xi_{m,l^{\prime},j}|\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi_{m,l^{\prime}}\cap\mathsf{supp}(\theta)\}\Big).

(E.200)

For the second term, we have with the definition of
{\mathcal{E}}_{m}(\epsilon)
that

\displaystyle\sum_{j}|\xi_{m,l,j}|\cdot|{\xi_{m,l^{\prime},j}}|\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi_{m,l}\cap\phi_{m,l^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{j,l}|\xi_{m,l,j}|^{2}\cdot|\phi_{m,l}\cap\phi_{m,l^{\prime}}|

(E.201)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{2}\log k.

(E.202)

For the last two terms, applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality gives, for
\ell\in\{l,l^{\prime}\}
, that

\displaystyle\gamma\sum_{j}|\theta_{j}|\cdot|\xi_{m,\ell,j}|\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi_{m,\ell}\cap\mathsf{supp}(\theta)\}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\|\theta\|_{2}\cdot\epsilon\sqrt{\log k}\leq\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log k.

(E.203)

Putting them together, we have that

\displaystyle|\langle{w_{m,l}},{w_{m,l^{\prime}}}\rangle|\leq 4(\gamma^{2}+\epsilon^{2}\log k).

(E.204)

This concludes the proof of
Lemma

E.3
.
∎

Proof of
Proposition

E.4
.

For conciseness, we momentarily drop the subscript
m,l
in the following analysis. Conditioning on fixed
\phi
, we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{w}[\langle{w},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{s}]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{w}\big[\|\gamma\theta+\xi\|_{2}^{-s}\cdot\big(\gamma\langle{\theta},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle+\langle{\xi},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle\big)^{s}\big]

(E.205)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\phi}\big[{\mathbb{E}}_{w}\big[\|\gamma\theta+\xi\|_{2}^{-s}\cdot\big(\gamma\rho+\langle{\xi},{P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}}\rangle\big)^{s}{\,\big|\,}\phi\big]\big].

(E.206)

Given the polarization level
\gamma=o(1)
, we see that
\|\gamma\theta+\xi\|_{2}^{s+1}\simeq 1\pm o(1)
, and it suffices to evaluate
{\mathbb{E}}_{w}\big[\big(\gamma\rho+\langle{\xi},{P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}}\rangle\big)^{s}{\,\big|\,}\phi\big]
.
Without loss of generality, we assume that
1\in\phi
and we can translate
P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}
into the first coordinate by the isotropy of
\xi
over
{\mathbb{S}}^{k-1}(\phi)
. To this end, we can characterize the first term as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\big[\big(\gamma\rho+\langle{\xi},{P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}}\rangle\big)^{s}{\,\big|\,}\phi\big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\big[\big(\gamma\rho+\langle{\xi},{\|P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}\|_{2}\cdot e_{1}}\rangle\big)^{s}{\,\big|\,}\phi\big]

(E.207)

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=0}^{s}\binom{2\lfloor s/2\rfloor}{r}(\gamma\rho)^{s-r}\cdot\|P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}\|_{2}^{r}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\big[\xi_{1}^{r}{\,\big|\,}\phi\big]\cdot\ind\{r\text{ even}\}

(E.208)

\displaystyle\overset{\textrm{(i)}}{\simeq}{}\sum_{r=0}^{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\binom{2\lfloor s/2\rfloor}{2r}(\gamma\rho)^{2\lfloor s/2\rfloor-2r}\cdot\|P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}\|_{2}^{2r}\cdot k^{-r}\cdot(\gamma\rho)^{\ind\{s\text{ odd}\}}

(E.209)

\displaystyle=(\gamma\rho)^{\ind\{s\text{ odd}\}}\cdot\big((\gamma\rho+k^{-1/2}\|P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}\|_{2})^{2\lfloor s/2\rfloor}+(\gamma\rho-k^{-1/2}\|P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}\|_{2})^{2\lfloor s/2\rfloor}\big)/2

(E.210)

\displaystyle\simeq(\gamma\rho)^{\ind\{s\text{ odd}\}}\cdot(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}\|P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}\|_{2})^{2\lfloor s/2\rfloor}.

(E.211)

Here, (i) holds by applying
Lemma

H.5
and
\simeq
denotes equality up to an
s
-dependent multiplicative constant.

Putting together Eq. (
E.206
) and (
E.211
),
we conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{w}[\langle{w},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{s}\,|\,\phi]

\displaystyle\simeq(\gamma\rho)^{\ind\{s\text{ odd}\}}(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}\|P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}\|_{2})^{s-\ind\{s\text{ odd}\}}.

(E.212)

In the sequel, we consider averaging over
\phi
. From Eq. (
E.212
), we see that it suffices to consider
{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi}[(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}\|P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}\|_{2})^{r}]
for some
r\geq 2
. We alter the notation to facilitate some deferred calculation. Consider
\mathbf{m}\subset[d]
with constant size
|\mathbf{m}|=O(1)
that does not scale with
k
or
d
. Now define
\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\sim{\text{Unif}}\{{\mathcal{S}}_{k,\mathbf{m}}\}
, where
{\mathcal{S}}_{k,\mathbf{m}}=\{S\subset[d]:|S|=k,\mathbf{m}\subset S\}
. It is easily seen that this definition covers previous definition of
{\mathcal{S}}_{k,m}
by setting
\mathbf{m}=\{m\}
. We characterize the magnitude of
{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}[\|P_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\theta^{\star}\|_{2}^{r}]
from both sides as follows. For the lower bound, we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}[\|P_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\theta^{\star}\|_{2}^{r}]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\Big[\big(\|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^{\star}\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{j\notin\mathbf{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{2}\ind\{j\in\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}\big)^{r/2}\Big]

(E.213)

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\Big[\|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^{\star}\|_{r}^{r}+\sum_{j\notin\mathbf{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{r}\ind\{j\in\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}\Big]

(E.214)

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(i)}{\simeq}(1-k/d)\cdot\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{r}^{r}+\frac{k}{d}\cdot\|\theta^{\star}\|_{r}^{r}

(E.215)

\displaystyle\overset{\rm(ii)}{\gtrsim}\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{r}^{r}+\frac{k}{d}\cdot k^{1-r/2}\cdot\|\theta^{\star}\|_{2}^{r/2}

(E.216)

\displaystyle=\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{r}^{r}+\frac{k^{2}}{d}\cdot k^{-r/2}.

(E.217)

Here (i) holds by the fact that
{\mathbb{E}}[\ind\{j\in\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}]\simeq k/d
for
j\notin\mathbf{m}
, and (ii) is a consequence of Jensen’s inequality.
For the upper bound, we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}[\|P_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\theta^{\star}\|_{2}^{r}]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\Big[\Big(\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{j\notin\mathbf{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{2}\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}\Big)^{r/2}\Big]

(E.218)

\displaystyle\lesssim{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\Big[\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{r}^{r}+\Big(\underbrace{\sum_{j\notin\mathbf{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{2}\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}}_{|(\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\cap\phi^{\star})\setminus\mathbf{m}|\text{ nonzero summands}}\Big)^{r/2}\Big]

(E.219)

\displaystyle\overset{\text{Jensen}}{\lesssim}\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{r}^{r}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\Big[|(\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\cap\phi^{\star})\setminus\mathbf{m}|^{r/2-1}\cdot\Big(\sum_{j\notin\mathbf{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{r}\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}\Big)\Big].

(E.220)

Next, we apply Cauchy-Schwarz inequality as follows:

(
E.220
)

\displaystyle=\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{r}^{r}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\Big[\sum_{j\notin\mathbf{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{r}\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}^{2}\cdot|(\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\cap\phi^{\star})\setminus\mathbf{m}|^{r/2-1}\Big]

(E.221)

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{r}^{r}+{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\Big[\sum_{j\notin\mathbf{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{2r}\cdot\ind\{j\in\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}\Big]^{1/2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\Big[\sum_{j\notin\mathbf{m}}\ind\{j\in\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\}\cdot|(\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\cap\phi^{\star})\setminus\mathbf{m}|^{r-2}\Big]^{1/2}

(E.222)

\displaystyle=\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{r}^{r}+\Big(\frac{k}{d}\cdot\sum_{j\notin\mathbf{m}}|\theta_{j}^{\star}|^{2r}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}\big[|(\phi_{\mathbf{m}}\cap\phi^{\star})\setminus\mathbf{m}|^{r-1}\big]^{1/2}

(E.223)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\lesssim}\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{r}^{r}+\Big(\frac{k}{d}\cdot k^{1-r}\Big)^{1/2}\cdot\Big(\frac{k^{2}}{d}\Big)^{(r-1)/2}

(E.224)

\displaystyle=\|\theta^{\star}_{\mathbf{m}}\|_{r}^{r}+\frac{k^{2}}{d}\cdot k^{-r/2},

(E.225)

where (i) holds by
{\mathcal{E}}_{0,2r}
and
Lemma

H.16
.
In conclusion, we have that
{\mathbb{E}}_{\phi_{\mathbf{m}}}[\|P_{\phi}\theta^{\star}\|_{2}^{r}]\simeq\|\theta_{\mathbf{m}}^{\star}\|_{r}^{r}+k^{-r/2}\cdot\delta
given that
k=o(\sqrt{d})
. Combining this result with Eq. (
E.212
), we obtain that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{w}[\langle{w},{\theta^{\star}}\rangle^{s}]

\displaystyle\simeq(\gamma\rho)^{\ind\{s\text{ odd}\}}(\gamma|\rho|+k^{-1/2}|\theta_{m}^{\star}|+k^{-1}\delta_{s-\ind\{s\text{ odd}\}})^{s-\ind\{s\text{ odd}\}}

(E.226)

where
\delta=k^{2}/d=o(1)
and
\delta_{r}=\delta^{1/r}
.
∎

Appendix F
Statistical Query Lower Bound for Sparse Signal Recovery

In this section, we provide a
k^{s^{\star}}
sample complexity lower bound for the single index model with
k
-sparse signal when querying a
\mathrm{VSTAT}
oracle. The statistical query (SQ) framework was developed in
26
and for completeness, we present essential definitions and results here.

Definition F.1

(
\mathrm{VSTAT}
Oracle)
.

Let
D^{\star}
be the input distribution over domain
{\mathcal{X}}
. For a sample size parameter
n>0
,
\mathrm{VSTAT}(D^{\star},n)
oracle is the oracle that for any query function
h:{\mathcal{X}}\rightarrow[0,1]
, returns a value
v\in[p-\tau,p+\tau]
, where
p={\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim D^{\star}}[h(x)]
and
\tau=\max\{n^{-1},\sqrt{p(1-p)/n}\}
.

To define a key concept
statistical query dimension
, we first introduce the following notation.

Definition F.2

(Relative Pairwise Correlation)
.

Given two distributions
D_{1},D_{2}\in\Delta({\mathcal{X}})
and a reference distribution
D\in\Delta({\mathcal{X}})
,

\displaystyle\chi_{D}(D_{1},D_{2})={\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim D}\left[\frac{D_{1}(x)}{D(x)}\cdot\frac{D_{2}(x)}{D(x)}\right]-1.

(F.1)

Definition F.3

(Statistical Dimension)
.

For
\widebar{\gamma}>0
,
\eta\in(0,1)
, domain
{\mathcal{X}}
, a set of distributions
{\mathcal{D}}
over
{\mathcal{X}}
,
the
statistical dimension

\mathrm{SDA}({\mathcal{D}},\widebar{\gamma},\eta)
of
{\mathcal{D}}
with average correlation
\widebar{\gamma}
and solution set bound
\eta
is defined as the largest value
m^{\prime}
such that there exists a
reference distribution

D\in\Delta({\mathcal{X}})
and a finite set of distributions
{\mathcal{D}}_{D}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}
which can depend on the reference
D
with the following property: for any solution
D^{\star}\in{\mathcal{D}}
,

(i)

|{\mathcal{D}}_{D}\setminus\{D^{\star}\}|\geq(1-\eta)|{\mathcal{D}}_{D}|
;

(ii)

for any subset
{\mathcal{D}}_{D}^{\prime}\subseteq{\mathcal{D}}_{D}\setminus\{D^{\star}\}
such that
|{\mathcal{D}}_{D}^{\prime}|\geq|{\mathcal{D}}_{D}\setminus\{D^{\star}\}|/m^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{D}}_{D}^{\prime}|^{2}}\sum_{D_{i},D_{j}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{D}^{\prime}}\chi_{D}(D_{i},D_{j})\leq\widebar{\gamma}.

(F.2)

The above definition of the statistical dimension is a special case of the original Definition 3.1 in
26
where we consider a search problem of
exact recovery
of the ground truth
D^{\star}
.

Definition F.4

(
(\gamma,\beta)
-correlated Distributions)
.

We say that a set of
m
distributions
{\mathcal{D}}=\{D_{1},\ldots,D_{m}\}
over
{\mathcal{X}}
is
(\gamma,\beta)
-correlated relative to a reference distribution
D\in\Delta({\mathcal{X}})
if:

\chi_{D}(D_{i},D_{j})\leq\begin{cases}\beta&\mbox{ for }i=j\in[m]\\
\gamma&\mbox{ for }i\not=j\in[m].\end{cases}

The following lemma borrowed from Lemma 3.10 of
26
provides a lower bound on the statistical dimension in terms of the
(\gamma,\beta)
-correlation property of the set of candidate distributions.

Lemma F.5
.

Given a set of candidate distributions
{\mathcal{D}}
that are
(\gamma,\beta)
-correlated with respect to a reference distribution
D
, then for any
\gamma^{\prime}>0
and
\eta>|{\mathcal{D}}|^{-1}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{SDA}({\mathcal{D}},\gamma+\gamma^{\prime},\eta)\geq\frac{(|{\mathcal{D}}|-1)\gamma^{\prime}}{\beta-\gamma}.

(F.3)

The main result in the SQ framework is the following statement that relates the number of queries required to the statistical dimension, which is borrowed from Theorem 3.2 of
26
.

Lemma F.6
.

Let
{\mathcal{X}}
be a domain and
{\mathcal{D}}
be a set of candidate distributions over
{\mathcal{X}}
. For any
\widebar{\gamma}>0
and
\eta\in(0,1)
, any randomized SQ algorithm that solves the problem of finding the input distribution
D^{\star}\in{\mathcal{D}}
with probability at least
\alpha>\eta
requires at least
(\alpha-\eta)/(1-\eta)\cdot\mathrm{SDA}({\mathcal{D}},\widebar{\gamma},\eta)
calls to the
\mathrm{VSTAT}(D^{\star},(3\widebar{\gamma})^{-1})
oracle.

Our strategy for proving the lower bound is to first construct a set of candidate distributions
{\mathcal{D}}
that are
(\omega(k^{-1}),\beta)
-correlated with respect to reference distribution
{\mathbb{Q}}
with
\beta=D_{\chi^{2}}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}\,\|\,{\mathbb{Q}})
and
|{\mathcal{D}}|
exponentially large.
Then by
Lemma

F.5
and
Lemma

F.6
, we can derive the desired hardness result.
It remains to construct the set of candidate distributions
{\mathcal{D}}
that are
(\omega(k^{-1}),\beta)
-correlated with respect to
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
To this end, we introduce the following result on the packing number of
k
-sparse vectors.

Lemma F.7

(Packing Number for
k
-Sparse Vectors)
.

Define
\rho(u,v)=|\langle u,v\rangle|
. Let packing number
{\mathcal{M}}_{\rho}(d,k,t)
be the maximal cardinality of the set of
k
-sparse vectors in
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
such that
\rho(u,v)<t
for any
u\neq v
in the set. We have for any
t\in(1/k,1)
that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{M}}_{\rho}(d,k,t)\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\exp\left(\frac{\min\left\{(d-k)t^{2}\>,\>{3kt}\right\}}{8}\right).

(F.4)

With all these ingredients in place, we are ready to prove the main theorem.

Proof of
Theorem

5.4
.

Let us pick parameter
\kappa_{d}\in((\log d)^{2},k/4)
that scales with
d
and set

\displaystyle t\geq\max\left\{\sqrt{\frac{\kappa_{d}}{d-k}},\frac{\kappa_{d}}{3k}\right\}\in(1/k,1/2).

(F.5)

Note that
t\in(1/k,1/2)
is able to hold by our choice of
\kappa_{d}
and condition that
\omega((\log d)^{2})\leq k\leq d/2
.
In this vein, we can pick
{\mathcal{D}}
to be the
maximal
set of distributions
{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}
for some
k
-sparse vectors
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
satisfying
\rho(\theta,\theta^{\prime})<t
for any
\theta\neq\theta^{\prime}
in the set.
It follows from plugging (
F.5
) into
Lemma

F.7
that
|{\mathcal{D}}|\geq\exp(\kappa_{d}/8)/2
, which is super polynomially large in
d
for our choice of
\kappa_{d}
.

Next, we configure the remaining parameters in
Lemma

F.5
and
Lemma

F.6
.
We choose the reference distribution to be
{\mathbb{Q}}
, in which the covariate
z
is independent of the output
y
.
For
\beta
, we note that

\displaystyle\chi_{{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta},{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta})=D_{\chi^{2}}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}\,\|\,{\mathbb{Q}})=O(1),

(F.6)

which is a constant independent of
\theta
due to the rotational invariance of the likelihood ratio with respect to
\theta
.
Thus, we denote this quantity by
B
and set
\beta=D_{\chi^{2}}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}\,\|\,{\mathbb{Q}})=B
.
For
\gamma
, we note that for any two
{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta},{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\prime}}
in
{\mathcal{D}}
for
\theta\neq\theta^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle|\chi_{\mathbb{Q}}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta},{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\prime}})|

\displaystyle=\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta}(x)}{{\mathbb{Q}}(x)}\cdot\frac{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\prime}}(x)}{{\mathbb{Q}}(x)}\right]-1\right|

(F.7)

\displaystyle=\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim{\mathbb{Q}}}\Biggl[\biggl(1+\sum_{s\geq s^{\star}}\zeta_{s}(y)h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)\biggr)\cdot\biggl(1+\sum_{s^{\prime}\geq s^{\star}}\zeta_{s^{\prime}}(y)h_{s^{\prime}}(\langle\theta^{\prime},z\rangle)\biggr)\Biggr]-1\right|

(F.8)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\geq s^{\star}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)^{2}]\cdot|\langle\theta,\theta^{\prime}\rangle|^{s}\leq\sum_{s\geq s^{\star}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)^{2}]\cdot t^{s}\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s^{\star}}(y)^{2}]\cdot t^{s^{\star}}+\frac{t^{s^{\star}+1}}{1-t}.

(F.9)

Here, the third equality follows from the fact that only when
s=s^{\prime}
, the cross term
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)h_{s}(\langle\theta^{\prime},z\rangle)]
is non-zero.
In particular, by the property of the Gaussian noise operator introduced in (
B.8
), we have that

{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)h_{s}(\langle\theta^{\prime},z\rangle)]=\langle\theta,\theta^{\prime}\rangle^{s}<t^{s}.

For the last inequality above, we simply use the fact that
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)^{2}]\leq 1
for any
s

(
17
)
and
t<1
.
Now, we conclude that

\displaystyle|\chi_{\mathbb{Q}}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta},{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\prime}})|\leq\left({\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s^{\star}}(y)^{2}]+\frac{t}{1-t}\right)\cdot t^{s^{\star}}\leq\left({\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s^{\star}}(y)^{2}]+1\right)\cdot t^{s^{\star}}.

(F.10)

We thus set
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma=\left({\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s^{\star}}(y)^{2}]+1\right)\cdot t^{s^{\star}}=\Theta(t^{s^{\star}})
.
Finally, we set
\eta=1/3
and
\alpha=2/3
.
Then all the conditions in both
Lemma

F.5
and
Lemma

F.6
are satisfied and we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{SDA}({\mathcal{D}},2\gamma,1/3)\geq\frac{(|{\mathcal{D}}|-1)\gamma}{\beta-\gamma}\geq\frac{|{\mathcal{D}}|\gamma}{2\beta}\geq\frac{\gamma\exp(\kappa_{d}/8)}{4\beta}.

(F.11)

Lastly, recall that we have
|\langle\theta,\theta^{\prime}\rangle|\leq t
for any
\theta\neq\theta^{\prime}
in
{\mathcal{D}}
, which means that in order to achieve alignment at least
2t
with the true signal
\theta^{\star}
, we need to
exactly
identify the distribution
{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}
.
Consequently, by
Lemma

F.6
, we have that any randomized SQ algorithm that solves the problem of achieving alignment
2t
with probability at least
2/3
requires at least

{\gamma\exp(\kappa_{d}/8)}/(8B)
calls to the
\mathrm{VSTAT}({\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}},(6\gamma)^{-1})
oracle.

Simplification of the lower bound

To simplify the lower bound, let us take
\kappa_{d}=(\log d)^{c}/2
for some constant
c>2
. Thus, the alignment
2t
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle 2t\leq\begin{cases}\widetilde{\omega}(k^{-1})&\mbox{ if }k<\sqrt{d}\\
\widetilde{\omega}(d^{-1/2})&\mbox{ if }k\geq\sqrt{d}\end{cases},

(F.12)

where
