\widetilde{\omega}(\cdot)
hides some poly-logarithmic factors.
The number of queries is still super polynomially large in
d
.
Following from (
F.5
), we can safely set

\displaystyle t=\begin{cases}(\log d)^{c}/k&\mbox{ if }(\log d)^{2}<k<\sqrt{d(\log d)^{c}}\\
\sqrt{(\log d)^{c}/d}&\mbox{ if }\sqrt{d(\log d)^{c}}\leq k\leq d/2\end{cases},

(F.13)

Hence, the sample size parameter

\displaystyle(6\gamma)^{-1}=\frac{t^{-s^{\star}}}{6}\simeq\begin{cases}\frac{k^{s^{\star}}}{(\log d)^{cs^{\star}}}&\mbox{ if }(\log d)^{2}<k<\sqrt{d(\log d)^{c}}\\
\frac{d^{s^{\star}/2}}{6(\log d)^{cs^{\star}/2}}&\mbox{ if }\sqrt{d(\log d)^{c}}\leq k\leq d/2\end{cases}.

(F.14)

Hence, we have established the desired lower bound on the sample complexity.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

F.7
.

We use the probability method to prove the existence of a set of
k
-sparse vectors in
\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
with the desired property.
We i.i.d. sample
m
vectors
\omega^{(1)},\ldots,\omega^{(m)}
from the following distribution:

\displaystyle\omega:\quad\phi\sim{\text{Unif}}({\mathcal{S}}_{k}),\quad\omega_{j}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}},&\mbox{ w.p. }\frac{1}{2}\mbox{ if }j\in\phi\\
-\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}},&\mbox{ w.p. }\frac{1}{2}\mbox{ if }j\in\phi\\
0,&j\notin\phi.\end{cases},\quad j\in[d].

(F.15)

where we recall that
{\mathcal{S}}_{k}
is the set of all size-
k
subsets in
[d]
.
Since each
\omega^{(i)}
is i.i.d. sampled,
we can equivalently view
\langle\omega^{(i)},\omega^{(j)}\rangle
for
i\neq j
as a random variable sampled from the following distribution:

\displaystyle\langle\omega^{(i)},\omega^{(j)}\rangle\overset{d}{=}\frac{R_{X}}{k},\quad\text{where}\quad R_{X}=r_{1}+\ldots,r_{X},\quad X\sim\mathrm{Hypergeometric}(d,k,k),

(F.16)

where
r_{1},r_{2},\ldots
are i.i.d. Rademacher random variables.
Let us consider random variable
W
distributed as

\displaystyle W\overset{d}{=}\frac{R_{Y}}{k},\quad\text{where}\quad R_{Y}=r_{1}+\ldots+r_{Y},\quad Y\sim\mathrm{Binomial}\Bigl(k,\frac{k}{d-k}\Bigr).

(F.17)

We will invoke the following fact on the tail probability regarding the above two random variables.

Proposition F.8
.

For
R_{X}
and
R_{Y}
defined in (
F.16
) and (
F.17
), respectively, we have that
{\mathbb{P}}(R_{X}\geq t)\leq 2{\mathbb{P}}(R_{Y}\geq t)
for any
t>1
.

The proof of the proposition is deferred to the end of the proof.
Thus, it suffices to study the tail probability of
W
.
Note that
W\overset{d}{=}\sum_{j=1}^{k}w_{j}
where
w_{j}
are i.i.d. sampled from

\displaystyle w_{j}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{k},&\mbox{ w.p. }\frac{k}{2(d-k)}\\
-\frac{1}{k},&\mbox{ w.p. }\frac{k}{2(d-k)}\\
0,&\mbox{ w.p. }1-\frac{k}{d-k}\end{cases},\quad j\in[k].

(F.18)

where
{\mathbb{E}}[w_{j}]=0
and
{\mathbb{E}}[w_{j}^{2}]=(k(d-k))^{-1}
.
Hence, we can apply the Bernstein inequality to obtain that for any
t>1/k
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\langle\omega^{(i)},\omega^{(j)}\rangle\geq t)

\displaystyle\leq 2{\mathbb{P}}(W\geq t)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{k(t/k)^{2}/2}{(k(d-k))^{-1}+t/(3k^{2})}\right)

(F.19)

\displaystyle=2\exp\left(-\frac{k^{2}t^{2}}{2k^{2}/(d-k)+2kt/3}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{(d-k)t^{2}}{4}\>,\>\frac{3kt}{4}\right\}\right).

(F.20)

Suppose we randomly sample
m
i.i.d.
\omega^{(i)}
from the same distribution.
Then the probability that all such pair
|\langle\omega^{(i)},\omega^{(j)}\rangle|<t
for
t>1/k
is lower bounded by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\bigl(|\langle\omega^{(i)},\omega^{(j)}\rangle|<t,\forall i\neq j\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq 1-m^{2}\cdot 2{\mathbb{P}}(\langle\omega^{(i)},\omega^{(j)}\rangle\geq t)

(F.21)

\displaystyle\geq 1-4m^{2}\cdot\exp\left(-\min\left\{\frac{(d-k)t^{2}}{4}\>,\>\frac{3kt}{4}\right\}\right).

(F.22)

Ensuring that the probability is nonzero will give us a valid construction of the set
{\mathcal{D}}
.
Therefore, there must exist a
{\mathcal{D}}
satisfying
|\langle\omega^{(i)},\omega^{(j)}\rangle|<t
for any
i\neq j
and with size

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{D}}|\geq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\exp\left(\frac{\min\left\{(d-k)t^{2}\>,\>{3kt}\right\}}{8}\right).

(F.23)

Hence, we complete the proof.
∎

Next, we aim to present the proof of
Proposition

F.8
. To proceed, let us introduce the definition of stochastic dominance.

Definition F.9

(Stochastic Dominance)
.

For any real-valued random variable
X
and
Y
, we say that
X
is
stochastically dominated
by
Y
, denoted by
X\preceq Y
, if
{\mathbb{P}}(X\geq t)\leq{\mathbb{P}}(Y\geq t)
for every
t
.

The following result is from Theorem A in Chapter 2 of
48
.

Proposition F.10
.

For random variables
X
and
Y
,
X\preceq Y
if and only if there exists a coupling
(\widehat{X},\widehat{Y})
with
\widehat{X}\overset{d}{=}X
and
\widehat{Y}\overset{d}{=}Y
such that
\widehat{X}\leq\widehat{Y}
almost surely.

Proposition F.11

(Theorem 1.1,
38
)
.

For any integers
d
and
k
, let
X\sim\mathrm{Hypergeometric}(d,k,k)
and
Y\sim\mathrm{Binomial}(k,k/(d-k))
.
Then
X\preceq Y
.

Another way to think of the problem is that
\mathrm{Hypergeometric}(d,k,k)
corresponds to the number of times a black ball is drawn when sampling for
k
times from an urn with
d-k
white balls and
k
black balls without replacement, while
\mathrm{Binomial}(k,k/(d-k))
corresponds to sampling in the same urn but with replacement.
We claim the following fact on the tail probability of sums of Rademacher random variables.

Proposition F.12

(Sum of Rademacher Random Variables)
.

Let
r_{1},r_{2},\ldots
be i.i.d. Rademacher random variables. Let
R_{l}=r_{1}+\ldots+r_{l}
for
l=1,2,\ldots
. Let
p_{l}(\cdot)
be the probability mass function of
R_{l}
. Then the following holds for any
l=1,2,\ldots
:

1.

p_{l}
is symmetric and supported on the set of odd integers if
l
is odd, and supported on the set of even integers if
l
is even.

2.

For
i\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(p_{l})
and
i\geq 0
,
p_{l}(i)
is a non-increasing function of
i
.

3.

{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t)\leq{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l+2}\geq t)
for any
t>1
.

4.

{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t)\leq 2{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l+1}\geq t)
for any
t>1
.

5.

{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t)\leq 2{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l+l^{\prime}}\geq t)
for any
l\geq 1
and
l^{\prime}\geq 1
.

Proof of
Proposition

F.12
.

The first claim is immediate from the symmetry of the Rademacher random variables and the fact that the sum of an odd number of Rademacher random variables is odd, while the sum of an even number of Rademacher random variables is even.
For the second claim, we note that

\displaystyle p_{l}(i)=2^{-l}\cdot\binom{l}{(i+l)/2},\quad i\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(p_{l}),

(F.24)

which is a non-increasing function for
i\geq 0
.
For the third claim, we let
t^{\star}=2{\lceil t/2\rceil}
if
l
is even and
t^{\star}=2{\lceil(t-1)/2\rceil}+1
if
l
is odd.
In other words,
t^{\star}=\min\{\tau\in\mathop{\mathrm{supp}}(p_{l}):\tau\geq t\}
.
Then we have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l+2}\geq t)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t^{\star}+2)+{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}=t^{\star})\cdot{\mathbb{P}}(r_{l+1}+r_{l+2}\geq 0)

(F.25)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}=t^{\star}-2)\cdot{\mathbb{P}}(r_{l+1}+r_{l+2}=2)

(F.26)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t^{\star})+\left({\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}=t^{\star}-2)-{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}=t^{\star})\right)\cdot{\mathbb{P}}(r_{l+1}+r_{l+2}=2)

(F.27)

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t^{\star})={\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t).

(F.28)

where in the first equality we use the fact that
r_{l+1}+r_{l+2}
is supported on
\{-2,0,2\}
and in the second equality we use the symmetric property of the distribution of
r_{l+1}+r_{l+2}
.
The last inequality follows from the monotonicity of the probability mass function of
R_{l}
for
t^{\star}-2\geq 0
when
t>1
.
For the fourth claim, we similarly have that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l+1}\geq t)

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t^{\star})-{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}=t^{\star})\cdot{\mathbb{P}}(r_{l+1}=-1)\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t^{\star})=\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t).

(F.29)

The last claim follows from a combination of the third and fourth claims where

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l+l^{\prime}}\geq t)\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l+2{\lfloor l^{\prime}/2\rfloor}}\geq t)\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l+2{\lfloor l^{\prime}/2\rfloor}-2}\geq t)\geq\ldots\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{P}}(R_{l}\geq t).

(F.30)

Hence, the proof is complete.
∎

Next, we proceed to the proof of
Proposition

F.8
.

Proof of
Proposition

F.8
.

By
Proposition

F.11
and
Proposition

F.10
, there exists a coupling
\widehat{X},\widehat{Y}
with
\mathop{\mathrm{law}}(\widehat{X})=\mathop{\mathrm{law}}(X)
and
\mathop{\mathrm{law}}(\widehat{Y})=\mathop{\mathrm{law}}(Y)
such that
\widehat{X}\leq\widehat{Y}
almost surely where
X\sim\mathrm{Hypergeometric}(d,k,k)
and
Y\sim\mathrm{Binomial}(k,k/(d-k))
.

Consider i.i.d. Rademacher random variables
r_{1},r_{2},\ldots,r_{k}
.
Let
R_{l}=r_{1}+\ldots+r_{l}
for
l=1,2,\ldots
.
Since
R_{\widehat{X}}=r_{1}+\ldots+r_{\widehat{X}}{\,|\,}\widehat{X}\overset{d}{=}2\mathrm{Binomial}(\widehat{X},1/2)-\widehat{X}
and
R_{\widehat{Y}}=r_{1}+\ldots+r_{\widehat{Y}}{\,|\,}\widehat{Y}\overset{d}{=}2\mathrm{Binomial}(\widehat{Y},1/2)-\widehat{Y}
for the coupling
(\widehat{X},\widehat{Y})
with
\widehat{X}\leq\widehat{Y}
, we consider the conditional random variable

\displaystyle r_{\widehat{X}+i}{\,|\,}(R_{\widehat{X}+i-1},\widehat{X},\widehat{Y})=r_{\widehat{X}+i}=\begin{cases}1,&\mbox{ w.p. }1/2\\
-1,&\mbox{ w.p. }1/2\end{cases},\quad i=1,2,\ldots,\widehat{Y}-\widehat{X}

(F.31)

The equality holds by the i.i.d. property of these Rademacher random variables.
From the distributional perspective, the distribution of
R_{\widehat{Y}}
is obtained by conducting convolution with the Rademacher distribution for
\widehat{Y}-\widehat{X}
times on the distribution of
R_{\widehat{X}}
. Invoking
Proposition

F.12
, we directly conclude that
{\mathbb{P}}(R_{\widehat{Y}}\geq t{\,|\,}\widehat{X},\widehat{Y})\geq{\mathbb{P}}(R_{\widehat{X}}\geq t{\,|\,}\widehat{X},\widehat{Y})/2
for any
t>1
and
\widehat{Y}\geq\widehat{X}
.
As
\widehat{Y}\geq\widehat{X}
holds almost surely, by the law of total probability, we arrive at the conclusion that
{\mathbb{P}}(R_{\widehat{Y}}\geq t)\geq{\mathbb{P}}(R_{\widehat{X}}\geq t)/2
for any
t>1
.
∎

Appendix G
Supporting Lemmas on Moment Calculations

Lemma G.1

(First moment)
.

Under
4.1
, for any
s\geq 0
, it holds for any
y\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
w,\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{d})}\big[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)z\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)\big]=\sqrt{s+1}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s+1}(y)\cdot\langle w,\theta\rangle^{s}w+\sqrt{s}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s-1}(y)\cdot\langle w,\theta\rangle^{s-1}\theta,

(G.1)

in the
L^{2}
sense over the marginal distribution of
y
under
{\mathbb{Q}}
.
Here and below, the second term is interpreted as zero when
s=0
.

Proof of
Lemma

G.1
.

For convenience, we denote
\rho:=\langle w,\theta\rangle
.
We first consider
|\rho|<1
; the cases
|\rho|=1
follow by continuity.
Decomposing
z
into the direction of
\theta
and the orthogonal complement of
\theta
, i.e.,
z=\langle\theta,z\rangle\theta+P_{\theta}^{\perp}z
where
P_{\theta}^{\perp}=I-{\theta}{\theta}^{\top}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)z\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\big[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\cdot\langle\theta,z\rangle\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)\big]\cdot{\theta}

(G.2)

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\big[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)\cdot P_{\theta}^{\perp}z\big].

(G.3)

Further note that
P_{\theta}^{\perp}z=\langle P_{\theta}^{\perp}z,P_{\theta}^{\perp}w\rangle P_{\theta}^{\perp}w/\|P_{\theta}^{\perp}w\|_{2}^{2}+P_{w}^{\perp}P_{\theta}^{\perp}z
where
P_{\theta}^{\perp}w=w-\rho\theta
and
\|P_{\theta}^{\perp}w\|_{2}^{2}=1-\rho^{2}
.
Then by symmetry of the Gaussian distribution, and plugging in
P_{\theta}^{\perp}z=z-\langle\theta,z\rangle\theta
, the second term on the right-hand side of (
G.3
) can be written as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\big[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)\cdot P_{\theta}^{\perp}z\big]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\big[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)\cdot\langle P_{\theta}^{\perp}z,w-\rho\theta\rangle\big]\cdot\frac{w-\rho\theta}{1-\rho^{2}}

(G.4)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\big[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\cdot\langle w,z\rangle\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)\big]\cdot\frac{w-\rho\theta}{1-\rho^{2}}

(G.5)

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\big[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\cdot\langle\theta,z\rangle\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)\big]\cdot\frac{-\rho w+\rho^{2}\theta}{1-\rho^{2}}

(G.6)

Plugging the above identity back into (
G.3
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)z\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)]

(G.7)

\displaystyle\quad=\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\big[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\cdot\langle\theta,z\rangle\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)\big]}_{\displaystyle\rm(I)}\cdot\frac{{\theta}-\rho w}{1-\rho^{2}}+\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\big[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\cdot\langle w,z\rangle\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)\big]}_{\displaystyle\rm(II)}\cdot\frac{w-\rho{\theta}}{1-\rho^{2}}.

(G.8)

Next, we analyze terms (I) and (II) in (
G.7
).

For convenience, we define the following Gaussian noise operator over the second argument of
\psi
:

\displaystyle{\mathrm{U}_{\rho}}\psi(y,x)={\mathbb{E}}_{x^{\prime}\sim{\mathcal{N}}}\big[\psi\big(y,\rho x+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}x^{\prime}\big)\big].

(G.9)

For term (I), writing
x=\langle\theta,z\rangle
and
x^{\prime}=\langle P_{\theta}^{\perp}w,z\rangle/\|P_{\theta}^{\perp}w\|_{2}
, it follows from
z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}
that
x,x^{\prime}\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}{\mathcal{N}}
.
Also,
\langle w,z\rangle=\rho x+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}x^{\prime}
.
Then we have

(I)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim{\mathcal{N}}}\left[{\mathrm{U}_{\rho}}\psi(y,x)\cdot x\cdot h_{s}(x)\right]={\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim{\mathcal{N}}}\left[\sqrt{s+1}\cdot{\mathrm{U}_{\rho}}\psi(y,x)\cdot h_{s+1}(x)+\sqrt{s}\cdot{\mathrm{U}_{\rho}}\psi(y,x)\cdot h_{s-1}(x)\right]

(G.10)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\overset{L^{2}({\mathbb{Q}})}{=}\sqrt{s+1}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s+1}(y)\cdot\rho^{s+1}+\sqrt{s}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s-1}(y)\cdot\rho^{s-1}.

(G.11)

where the second equality follows from the recurrence relation of the Hermite polynomials in (
B.3
),
and the third equality follows from the property of the Gaussian noise operator in (
B.8
).
Similarly for term (II), we apply (
B.8
) to obtain

(II)

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim{\mathcal{N}}}\left[{\mathrm{U}_{\rho}}\left(\psi(y,x)x\right)\cdot h_{s}(x)\right]\overset{L^{2}({\mathbb{Q}})}{=}\rho^{s}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim{\mathcal{N}}}\left[\psi(y,x)\cdot x\cdot h_{s}(x)\right]

(G.12)

\displaystyle\!\!\!\!\overset{L^{2}({\mathbb{Q}})}{=}\rho^{s}\cdot\left(\sqrt{s+1}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s+1}(y)+\sqrt{s}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s-1}(y)\right),

(G.13)

where the last equality again follows from the recurrence relation of the Hermite polynomials in (
B.3
).
Finally, plugging (
G.11
) and (
G.13
) into (
G.3
),

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)z\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta,z\rangle)]

\displaystyle\overset{L^{2}({\mathbb{Q}})}{=}\left(\sqrt{s+1}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s+1}(y)\cdot\rho^{s+1}+\sqrt{s}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s-1}(y)\cdot\rho^{s-1}\right)\cdot\frac{{\theta}-\rho w}{1-\rho^{2}}

(G.14)

\displaystyle\qquad+\rho^{s}\cdot\left(\sqrt{s+1}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s+1}(y)+\sqrt{s}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s-1}(y)\right)\cdot\frac{w-\rho{\theta}}{1-\rho^{2}}

(G.15)

\displaystyle\overset{~~~~~~}{=}\sqrt{s+1}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s+1}(y)\cdot\rho^{s}w+\sqrt{s}\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s-1}(y)\cdot\rho^{s-1}{\theta},

(G.16)

which completes the proof.
∎

An implication of the previous lemma is that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[h_{s^{\star}}(\langle\theta^{\star},z\rangle)\cdot\sigma^{\prime}(\langle z,\theta\rangle)\cdot\langle z,\theta^{\star}\rangle]=s^{\star}\cdot\widehat{\sigma}^{(s^{\star})}\cdot\langle\theta^{\star},\theta\rangle^{s^{\star}-1}+\sqrt{(s^{\star}+1)(s^{\star}+2)}\cdot\widehat{\sigma}^{(s^{\star}+2)}\cdot\langle\theta^{\star},\theta\rangle^{s^{\star}+1},

(G.17)

where we take
\widehat{\sigma}^{(s)}
as the
s
-th normalized Hermite coefficient of
\sigma
.
Here, we take
\psi(y,x)
as
\sigma^{\prime}(x)
and thus
\widehat{\psi}_{s}(y)=\sqrt{s+1}\cdot\widehat{\sigma}^{(s+1)}
.

Lemma G.2

(Decomposition of first-order moment)
.

Under
4.1
, define

\displaystyle g=\frac{1}{nL}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\big(\psi(y_{i},\langle w_{l},z_{i}\rangle)\cdot z_{i}-\widehat{\psi}_{1}(y_{i})\cdot w_{l}\big),

(G.18)

where
(z_{1},y_{1}),\ldots,(z_{n},y_{n})\overset{\mathrm{i.i.d.}}{\sim}{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}
and
w_{1},\ldots,w_{L}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
are fixed vectors.
Then it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[g]

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\geq s^{\star}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s-1}(y)]\cdot\frac{\sqrt{s}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\langle w_{l},\theta^{\star}\rangle^{s-1}\cdot\theta^{\star}

(G.19)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{s\geq s^{\star}}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)\cdot\widehat{\psi}_{s+1}(y)]\cdot\frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\langle w_{l},\theta^{\star}\rangle^{s}\cdot w_{l}.

(G.20)

Proof of
Lemma

G.2
.

Applying a change of measure from
{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}
to
{\mathbb{Q}}
and invoking (
2.2
), we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[g]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w_{l},z\rangle)\cdot z-\widehat{\psi}_{1}(y)\cdot w_{l}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\bigg[\psi(y,\langle w_{l},z\rangle)z\cdot\bigg(1+\sum_{s\geq s^{\star}}\zeta_{s}(y)h_{s}(\langle\theta^{\star},z\rangle)\bigg)-\widehat{\psi}_{1}(y)w_{l}\bigg]

(G.21)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sum_{s\geq s^{\star}}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\zeta_{s}(y)\cdot\psi(y,\langle w_{l},z\rangle)\cdot z\cdot h_{s}(\langle\theta^{\star},z\rangle)]+\frac{1}{L}\sum_{l=1}^{L}{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w_{l},z\rangle)z-\widehat{\psi}_{1}(y)w_{l}\right].

(G.22)

Applying
Lemma

G.1
to each term in the above expression completes the proof.
∎

Lemma G.3

(Second moment on nice event)
.

Suppose
\psi:{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
satisfies
4.1
with generative exponent
s^{\star}\geq 1
.
Suppose
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)^{2}]\leq C
for some universal
C=O(1)
and for all
s\geq s^{\star}
.
For
\epsilon,\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1/2)
satisfying
4e\epsilon^{2}<1/2
, consider any
w,w^{\prime},\theta^{\star},v\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
such that

\displaystyle\max\{|\langle\theta^{\star},w\rangle|,|\langle\theta^{\star},w^{\prime}\rangle|\}\leq\epsilon,\quad|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|\leq\epsilon_{1}

Also denote
\epsilon_{0}:=\max\{|\langle v,w\rangle|,|\langle v,w^{\prime}\rangle|\}\in[0,1]
.
Then it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\langle v,z\rangle^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\ind(s^{\star}=1)+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\epsilon_{0}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}+\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-2}+\ind(s^{\star}\geq 4)\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-3}

(G.23)

\displaystyle\qquad+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}+|\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle|\big(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\cdot\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-2}\big),

(G.24)

where
\lesssim
hides constants that only depend on
s^{\star}
,
{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\psi(y,x)^{4}]
and
C
.
Moreover, if we further suppose that either
v=\theta^{\star}
or
v\perp\theta^{\star}
, then for
s^{\star}\geq 2
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\langle v,z\rangle^{2}\right]

(G.25)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\bigg(1+\frac{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}+\epsilon\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)^{s^{\star}-1}+\ind(v\perp\theta^{\star})\cdot\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)^{s^{\star}-2}\cdot\frac{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}\cdot(\epsilon^{2}+\epsilon\cdot\ind(s^{\star}\geq 4))\bigg).

(G.26)

Proof.

For convenience, we denote the target quantity as
F:={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\langle v,z\rangle^{2}]
.
For
s^{\star}=1
, by
(c)
, we have

\displaystyle|F|\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)^{4}]^{1/4}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)^{4}]^{1/4}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{P}}_{\theta^{\star}}}[\langle v,z\rangle^{4}]^{1/2}=O(1).

(G.27)

For the general case
s^{\star}\geq 2
, we apply the likelihood ratio decomposition in (
2.2
) to get

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\langle v,z\rangle^{2}\right]+\sum_{s=s^{\star}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\langle v,z\rangle^{2}h_{s}(\langle\theta^{\star},z\rangle)\right].

(G.28)

Moreover, we decompose each of the terms using the following identity

\displaystyle h_{s}(\langle\theta^{\star},z\rangle)\langle v,z\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{(s+2)(s+1)}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s+2}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s}\otimes v^{\otimes 2}]+{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s}]

(G.29)

\displaystyle\qquad+2s\cdot\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s-1}\otimes v]+\sqrt{s(s-1)}\cdot\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle^{2}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s-2}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s-2}],

(G.30)

which is derived from (
B.10
) and
Proposition

H.1
, and we get

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle=\underbrace{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)(\sqrt{2}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{2}(z)[v^{\otimes 2}]+1)\right]}_{F_{1}}

(G.31)

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\sum_{s=s^{\star}}^{\infty}\sqrt{(s+2)(s+1)}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s+2}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s}\otimes v^{\otimes 2}]\right]}_{F_{2}}

(G.32)

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\sum_{s=s^{\star}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s}]\right]}_{F_{3}}

(G.33)

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\sum_{s=s^{\star}}^{\infty}2s\cdot\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes(s-1)}\otimes v]\right]}_{F_{4}}

(G.34)

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{\sum_{s=s^{\star}}^{\infty}\sqrt{s(s-1)}\cdot\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle^{2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s-2}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes(s-2)}]\right]}_{F_{5}}.

(G.35)

We bound each term
F_{1},F_{2},F_{3},F_{4},F_{5}
separately.

First, for the term
F_{1}
in (
G.35
),
we apply
Lemma

H.3
with test tensor
T_{2}=v^{\otimes 2}
and
c_{0}=2
to get

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{2}(z)[v^{\otimes 2}]\right]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\epsilon_{0}^{2}

(G.36)

where
\lesssim
hides constants that only depend on
s^{\star}
and
{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\psi(y,x)^{4}]
.
Similarly, applying
Lemma

H.3
for
{\bm{h}}_{0}(z)[1]=1
, we have
|{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)]|\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle|F_{1}|=\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\langle v,z\rangle^{2}\right]\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\epsilon_{0}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}.

(G.37)

Then for the term
F_{2}
in (
G.35
) corresponding to
\sqrt{(s+2)(s+1)}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s+2}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s}\otimes v^{\otimes 2}]
,
we apply
Proposition

H.4
with test tensor
T_{s}=v^{\otimes 2}\otimes(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes(s-2)},c_{0}=2
and
s_{0}=s^{\star}+2
to get that for
s^{\star}\geq 2
,

\displaystyle|F_{2}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\sum_{s=s^{\star}}^{\infty}\sqrt{(s+2)(s+1)}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s+2}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s}\otimes v^{\otimes 2}]\right]\bigg|

(G.38)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-2}+\ind(s^{\star}\geq 4)\cdot\big(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-3}\big).

(G.39)

Next for the term
F_{3}
in (
G.35
) corresponding to
{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s}]
, we apply
Proposition

H.4
with test tensor
T_{s}=(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s},c_{0}=0
and
s_{0}=s^{\star}
to get that for
s^{\star}\geq 2
,

\displaystyle|F_{3}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\sum_{s=s^{\star}}^{\infty}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s}]\right]\bigg|

(G.40)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon^{2s^{\star}}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}.

(G.41)

Next for the term
F_{4}
in (
G.35
) corresponding to
2s\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s-1}\otimes v]
, we apply
Proposition

H.4
with test tensor
T_{s}=v\otimes(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes(s-1)},c_{0}=1
and
s_{0}=s^{\star}
to get

\displaystyle|F_{4}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\sum_{s=s^{\star}}^{\infty}2s\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes(s-1)}\otimes v]\right]\bigg|

(G.42)

\displaystyle\lesssim|\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle|\big(\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\cdot\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-2}\big)\lesssim|\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle|\big(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\cdot\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-2}\big).

(G.43)

For the last term
F_{5}
in (
G.35
) corresponding to
\sqrt{s(s-1)}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s-2}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s-2}]
, we reindex by
r=s-2
and apply
Proposition

H.4
with test tensor
T_{r}=(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes r},c_{0}=0
and
s_{0}=s^{\star}-2
to get

\displaystyle|F_{5}|

\displaystyle=\bigg|\sum_{s=s^{\star}}^{\infty}\sqrt{s(s-1)}\cdot\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle^{2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s-2}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s-2}]\right]\bigg|

(G.44)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle^{2}\big(\ind(s^{\star}=2)\cdot\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}\big)\lesssim\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle^{2}\big(\ind(s^{\star}=2)\cdot\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}\big).

(G.45)

Combining the above bounds and rearranging the terms, we obtain

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\epsilon_{0}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}\big)+\big(\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-2}+\ind(s^{\star}\geq 4)\cdot(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-3})\big)+\big(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}\big)

(G.46)

\displaystyle\quad+|\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle|\big(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\cdot\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-2}+\ind(s^{\star}=2)\cdot\epsilon_{1}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}\big)

(G.47)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\epsilon_{0}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}+\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-2}+\ind(s^{\star}\geq 4)\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-3}+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}+|\langle\theta^{\star},v\rangle|\big(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\cdot\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon_{0}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-2}\big).

(G.48)

If
v=\theta^{\star}
, then we additionally have
\epsilon_{0}=\epsilon
, which simplifies the above bound to

\displaystyle F{\,|\,}_{s^{\star}\geq 2,v=\theta^{\star}}\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\epsilon^{2}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}=\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\bigg(1+\frac{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}+\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\epsilon\bigg).

(G.49)

For
v\perp\theta^{\star}
, we have

\displaystyle F{\,|\,}_{s^{\star}\geq 2,v\perp\theta^{\star}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-2}\epsilon_{0}^{2}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}+\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-2}+\ind(s^{\star}\geq 4)\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{2}\cdot\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-3}+\epsilon^{2s^{\star}-1}

(G.50)

\displaystyle\lesssim\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1}\bigg(1+\frac{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}+\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)^{s^{\star}-1}\cdot\epsilon+\left(\frac{\epsilon^{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}\right)^{s^{\star}-2}\cdot\frac{\epsilon_{0}^{2}}{\epsilon_{1}}\cdot\big(\epsilon^{2}+\epsilon\cdot\ind(s^{\star}\geq 4)\big)\bigg).

(G.51)

Hence, we complete the proof.
∎

Lemma G.4
.

For polarization level
\gamma\in(0,1/2)
and
e_{1}=(1,0,\ldots,0)^{\top}
, consider the polarized random vector

w=\frac{\gamma e_{1}+\xi}{\|\gamma e_{1}+\xi\|_{2}},\quad\text{where}\quad\xi\sim{\text{Unif}}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1}),

(G.52)

For
\rho\in[0,1]
, let
\theta^{\star}=(\rho,\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}},0,\ldots,0)\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
be a fixed direction.
Then, for
s\geq 1
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\langle\theta^{\star},w\rangle^{s}]\simeq\begin{cases}\displaystyle\left(|\rho|(\gamma+d^{-1/2})+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}d^{-1/2}\right)^{s}&\text{if $s$ is even}\\
\displaystyle\rho\gamma\left(|\rho|(\gamma+d^{-1/2})+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}d^{-1/2}\right)^{s-1}&\text{if $s$ is odd}\end{cases}

(G.53)

where
\simeq
hides constants that only depend on
s
.

Proof of
Lemma

G.4
.

Expanding
\langle\theta^{\star},w\rangle^{s}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\langle\theta^{\star},w\rangle^{s}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{s}\binom{s}{j}\rho^{j}(1-\rho^{2})^{(s-j)/2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}[w_{1}^{j}w_{2}^{s-j}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{s}\binom{s}{j}\rho^{j}(1-\rho^{2})^{(s-j)/2}\cdot\ind(\text{$s-j$ even})\cdot{\mathbb{E}}[w_{1}^{j}w_{2}^{s-j}]

(G.54)

where the second equality follows from
Lemma

H.9
.
Next, we consider the case of even
s
and odd
s
separately.

For even
s\geq 1
,
s-j
is even if and only if
j
is even, and thus we can rewrite (
G.54
) as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\langle\theta^{\star},w\rangle^{s}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{s/2}\binom{s}{2j}\rho^{2j}(1-\rho^{2})^{(s-2j)/2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}[w_{1}^{2j}w_{2}^{s-2j}].

Now applying
Lemma

H.9
again, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[w_{1}^{2j}w_{2}^{s-2j}]=C(2je_{1}+(s-2j)e_{2},\gamma)\cdot\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{2j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-2j}\simeq\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{2j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-2j}.

where
\simeq
hides constants that only depend on
s
.
Therefore, we further have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\langle\theta^{\star},w\rangle^{s}]

\displaystyle\simeq\sum_{j=0}^{s/2}\binom{s}{2j}\rho^{2j}(1-\rho^{2})^{(s-2j)/2}\cdot\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{2j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-2j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{s/2}\binom{s}{2j}\bigg(|\rho|\gamma+\frac{|\rho|}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{2j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-2j}

\displaystyle\simeq\Big(|\rho|(\gamma+d^{-1/2})+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}d^{-1/2}\Big)^{s}

where the last equality is due to
Lemma

H.13
.

The case for odd
s\geq 1
is similar.
Here
s-j
is even if and only if
j
is odd, and then it follows from
Lemma

H.9
and
Lemma

H.13
that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\mathbb{E}}[\langle\theta^{\star},w\rangle^{s}]&=\sum_{j=0}^{(s-1)/2}\binom{s}{2j+1}\rho^{2j+1}(1-\rho^{2})^{(s-2j-1)/2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}[w_{1}^{2j+1}w_{2}^{s-2j-1}]\\
&\simeq\rho\sum_{j=0}^{(s-1)/2}\binom{s}{2j+1}\rho^{2j}(1-\rho^{2})^{(s-2j-1)/2}\cdot\gamma\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{2j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-2j-1}\\
&\simeq\rho\gamma\Big(|\rho|(\gamma+d^{-1/2})+\sqrt{1-\rho^{2}}d^{-1/2}\Big)^{s-1}\end{aligned}

where
\simeq
hides constants that only depend on
s
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix H
Technical Results

H.1
Technical Results for Hermite Tensor

Proposition H.1
.

Let
s\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
.
Then for any
z\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
and
i,j\in[d]
,

\displaystyle z_{i}z_{j}{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Sym}\Bigl(\sqrt{(s+2)(s+1)}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s+2}(z)[e_{i}\otimes e_{j}]+\delta_{ij}{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)+s\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[e_{j}]\otimes e_{i}

(H.1)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+s\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[e_{i}]\otimes e_{j}+\sqrt{s(s-1)}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s-2}(z)\otimes e_{i}\otimes e_{j}\Bigr),

(H.2)

where
e_{i}
and
e_{j}
are the
i
-th and
j
-th canonical basis vectors in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
respectively.
Here by convention,
{\bm{h}}_{-1}(z)
and
{\bm{h}}_{-2}(z)
are always zero tensors.

Proof of
Proposition

H.1
.

Note that each element of
h_{s}(\langle z,\theta^{\star}\rangle)zz^{\top}
is a polynomial of the entries of
z
of degree at most
s+2
.
Therefore, it suffices to verify (
H.1
) by considering the product of
h_{s}(\langle z,\theta^{\star}\rangle)z_{i}z_{j}
with any monomial of degree at most
s+2
, under the expectation of
z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}
.
Consider any such test function
F:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
being a monomial of degree at most
s+2
, and we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[F(z)h_{s}(\langle z,\theta^{\star}\rangle)z_{i}z_{j}]={\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[F(z)z_{i}z_{j}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[(\theta^{\star})^{\otimes s}]]

(H.3)

where we apply (
B.10
) to rewrite the Hermite polynomial in terms of the Hermite tensor.
Since
F
is a monomial, it is infinitely differentiable, and moreover, all derivatives of
F
are square-integrable with respect to the standard normal distribution.
Then by Stein’s lemma for Hermite tensor (
B.11
),

\displaystyle\sqrt{s!}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[F(z)z_{i}z_{j}{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)]={\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}(F(z)z_{i}z_{j})]

(H.4)

\displaystyle\quad=\mathtt{Sym}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)z_{i}z_{j}]+s\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s-1}F(z)\otimes\nabla(z_{i}z_{j})]+\frac{s(s-1)}{2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s-2}F(z)\otimes\nabla^{2}(z_{i}z_{j})]\right).

(H.5)

Here we recall the symmetrization operator
\mathtt{Sym}
defined in (
B.2
).
Further apply Stein’s lemma to
{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)z_{i}z_{j}]
, and we get

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)z_{i}z_{j}]

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Sym}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)\delta_{ij}]+{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s+1}F(z)[e_{j}]z_{i}]\right)

(H.6)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Sym}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)\delta_{ij}]+{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s+2}F(z)[e_{i}\otimes e_{j}]]\right).

(H.7)

Similarly, we apply Stein’s lemma to
{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s-1}F(z)\otimes\nabla(z_{i}z_{j})]
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathtt{Sym}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s-1}F(z)\nabla(z_{i}z_{j})]\right)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Sym}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[z_{j}\nabla^{s-1}F(z)\otimes e_{i}+z_{i}\nabla^{s-1}F(z)\otimes e_{j}]\right)

(H.8)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Sym}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)[e_{j}]\otimes e_{i}]+{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)[e_{i}]\otimes e_{j}]\right).

(H.9)

Finally, for the third term in (
H.5
), it follows from
\nabla^{2}(z_{i}z_{j})=e_{i}\otimes e_{j}+e_{j}\otimes e_{i}
that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s-2}F(z)\otimes\nabla^{2}(z_{i}z_{j})]={\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s-2}F(z)\otimes(e_{i}\otimes e_{j}+e_{j}\otimes e_{i})].

(H.10)

Now, plugging (
H.7
), (
H.9
), and (
H.10
) back into (
H.5
), we obtain the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\sqrt{s!}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[F(z)z_{i}z_{j}{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)]

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Sym}\left({\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)\delta_{ij}]+{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s+2}F(z)[e_{i}\otimes e_{j}]]\right)

(H.11)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathtt{Sym}\left(s\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)[e_{j}]\otimes e_{i}]+s\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)[e_{i}]\otimes e_{j}]\right)

(H.12)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathtt{Sym}\left(s(s-1)\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s-2}F(z)\otimes e_{i}\otimes e_{j}]\right).

(H.13)

Now, applying Stein’s lemma (
B.11
) to relate the derivatives of
F
back to the Hermite tensor, i.e.,
{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\nabla^{s}F(z)]=\sqrt{s!}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[F(z){\bm{h}}_{s}(z)]
, it follows from (
H.13
) that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[F(z)z_{i}z_{j}{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\Bigl[F(z)\cdot\mathtt{Sym}\Bigl(\delta_{ij}{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)+\sqrt{(s+2)(s+1)}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s+2}(z)[e_{i}\otimes e_{j}]

(H.14)

\displaystyle\qquad+s\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[e_{j}]\otimes e_{i}+s\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[e_{i}]\otimes e_{j}+\sqrt{s(s-1)}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s-2}(z)\otimes e_{i}\otimes e_{j}\Bigr)\Bigr].

(H.15)

Since the monomial
F
is arbitrary, we conclude that

\displaystyle z_{i}z_{j}{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)

\displaystyle=\mathtt{Sym}\Bigl(\sqrt{(s+2)(s+1)}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s+2}(z)[e_{i}\otimes e_{j}]+\delta_{ij}{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)+s\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[e_{j}]\otimes e_{i}

(H.16)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+s\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[e_{i}]\otimes e_{j}+\sqrt{s(s-1)}\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s-2}(z)\otimes e_{i}\otimes e_{j}\Bigr).

(H.17)

This completes the proof.
∎

Proposition H.2
.

Let
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and
s\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
.
For any nonnegative integers
i,j
, if
|i-j|\leq s\leq i+j
and
s\equiv i-j\mod 2
, then

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[h_{i}(\langle w,z\rangle)h_{j}(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)\right]

(H.18)

\displaystyle\qquad=\binom{s}{(i-j+s)/2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{i!j!}{s!}}\cdot\frac{\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle^{(i+j-s)/2}}{((i+j-s)/2)!}\cdot\mathtt{Sym}\bigl(w^{\otimes(i-j+s)/2}\otimes{w^{\prime}}^{\otimes(j-i+s)/2}\bigr).

(H.19)

Otherwise
{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[h_{i}(\langle w,z\rangle)h_{j}(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)\right]
is the zero tensor.

Proof of
Proposition

H.2
.

By Stein’s lemma for Hermite tensors (
B.11
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[h_{i}(\langle w,z\rangle)h_{j}(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{s!}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\nabla_{z}^{s}\big(h_{i}(\langle w,z\rangle)h_{j}(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\big)\right]

(H.20)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{s!}}\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{s}\binom{s}{\tau}{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\mathtt{Sym}\left(\nabla_{z}^{\tau}h_{i}(\langle w,z\rangle)\otimes\nabla_{z}^{s-\tau}h_{j}(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\right)\right],

(H.21)

where we recall the
\mathtt{Sym}
operator defined in (
B.2
).
Further applying the recurrence relation of Hermite polynomials in (
B.3
) yields

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[h_{i}(\langle w,z\rangle)h_{j}(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)\right]

(H.22)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\sqrt{s!}}\cdot\sum_{\tau=0}^{s}\binom{s}{\tau}\sqrt{\frac{i!j!}{(i-\tau)!(j-s+\tau)!}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[h_{i-\tau}(\langle w,z\rangle)h_{j-s+\tau}(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\right]\cdot\mathtt{Sym}\bigl(w^{\otimes\tau}\otimes{w^{\prime}}^{\otimes s-\tau}\bigr)

(H.23)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{\tau=0}^{s}\ind(j=i+s-2\tau,i\geq\tau)\cdot\frac{\binom{s}{\tau}}{(i-\tau)!}\sqrt{\frac{i!j!}{s!}}\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle^{i-\tau}\mathtt{Sym}\bigl(w^{\otimes\tau}\otimes{w^{\prime}}^{\otimes s-\tau}\bigr)

(H.24)

where the second equality follows from (
B.8
).
Therefore, the above summation can be non-zero only if
|i-j|\leq s\leq i+j
and
s\equiv i-j\mod 2
, in which case the only non-zero term is when
\tau=(i-j+s)/2
.
This gives the desired result.
∎

Lemma H.3
.

Let
\psi:{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
such that
\psi^{2}\in L^{2}({\mathcal{N}}(0,1))
.
For some integer
s^{\star}\geq 1
, suppose that
\psi
is high-pass in the sense that the coefficient for its Hermite expansion
\widehat{\psi}_{i}=0
for any
i<s^{\star}-1
.
For any
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1/2)
, fix any
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
such that
|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|\leq\epsilon_{1}
, and consider a series of test tensors
\{T_{s}=v_{1}\otimes v_{2}\otimes\ldots\otimes v_{s}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes s}\}_{s=0}^{\infty}
such that
\sup_{i>c_{0}}\{|\langle w,v_{i}\rangle|\lor|\langle w^{\prime},v_{i}\rangle|\}\leq\epsilon

for some
\epsilon\in(0,1/2)
and integer
c_{0}\geq 0
.
Define
\epsilon_{0}\defeq\max_{1\leq i\leq c_{0}}\{|\langle w,v_{i}\rangle|\lor|\langle w^{\prime},v_{i}\rangle|\}
.
Then for any
s\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
, it holds that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\bigl[\psi(\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[T_{s}]\bigr]\right|\leq 4\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}\left(4es^{\star}\right)^{s/2}\sqrt{s+s^{\star}}\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{(s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor})\lor 0}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0},

(H.25)

where
\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}:={\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim{\mathcal{N}}}[\psi^{2}(x)]
.

Proof of
Lemma

H.3
.

Since
\psi^{2}\in L^{2}({\mathcal{N}}(0,1))
, we know that
\psi(\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\in L^{2}({\mathcal{N}}_{d})
as a function of
z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}
for any fixed
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
.
Therefore, we can apply the Hermite expansion of
\psi
to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\psi(\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)\right]=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\widehat{\psi}_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{j}h_{i}(\langle w,z\rangle)h_{j}(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)\right].

(H.26)

Further applying
Proposition

H.2
and restricting the summation over
j=0,1,\ldots
to
j=i+s-2\tau
for
\tau=0,1,\ldots,s
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\psi(\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)\right]

(H.27)

\displaystyle\qquad=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\tau=0}^{s}\ind(i\geq\tau)\cdot\binom{s}{\tau}\sqrt{\frac{1}{s!}}\frac{\sqrt{i!(i+s-2\tau)!}}{(i-\tau)!}\widehat{\psi}_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i+s-2\tau}\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle^{i-\tau}\cdot\mathtt{Sym}\bigl(w^{\otimes\tau}\otimes{w^{\prime}}^{\otimes(s-\tau)}\bigr),

(H.28)

Note that the double sums are interchangeable only if the series converges for each
\tau
, which can be verified using our assumptions:
For the test tensor
T_{s}=v_{1}\otimes v_{2}\otimes\ldots\otimes v_{s}\in({\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\otimes s}
, it holds that
|w^{\otimes\tau}\otimes w^{\prime\otimes(s-\tau)}[T_{s}]|=|\prod_{i=1}^{\tau}\langle w,v_{i}\rangle\prod_{j=1}^{s-\tau}\langle w^{\prime},v_{\tau+j}\rangle|\leq(\epsilon\vee\epsilon_{0})^{s}
, and similarly we have
|\mathtt{Sym}(w^{\otimes\tau}\otimes w^{\prime\otimes(s-\tau)})[T_{s}]|\leq(\epsilon\vee\epsilon_{0})^{s}
.
Then, since
|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|\leq\epsilon_{1}
where
\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1/2)
,

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{i=\tau}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{i!(i+s-2\tau)!}}{(i-\tau)!}\widehat{\psi}_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i+s-2\tau}\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle^{i-\tau}\cdot\mathtt{Sym}\bigl(w^{\otimes\tau}\otimes{w^{\prime}}^{\otimes(s-\tau)}\bigr)[T_{s}]\bigg|

(H.29)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\sum_{i=\tau}^{\infty}(i+s)^{s/2}\cdot\left|\widehat{\psi}_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i+s-2\tau}\right|\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{i-\tau}\cdot(\epsilon\vee\epsilon_{0})^{s}<\infty,

(H.30)

where we note that the exponential decay of
\epsilon_{1}^{i-\tau}
will dominate the polynomial growth of
(i+s)^{s/2}
, and that
\widehat{\psi}_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i+s-2\tau}
is uniformly bounded because
\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\widehat{\psi}_{i}^{2}<\infty
.
Hence, we can exchange the order of summation in (
H.27
) to obtain

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\psi(\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[T_{s}]\right]

(H.31)

\displaystyle\quad=\sum_{\tau=0}^{s}\binom{s}{\tau}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s!}}\cdot\mathtt{Sym}\bigl(w^{\otimes\tau}\otimes{w^{\prime}}^{\otimes(s-\tau)}\bigr)[T_{s}]\cdot\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle^{i_{\tau}-\tau}\underbrace{\sum_{i=i_{\tau}}^{\infty}\frac{\sqrt{i!(i+s-2\tau)!}}{(i-\tau)!}\widehat{\psi}_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i+s-2\tau}\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle^{i-i_{\tau}}}_{A_{\tau}}.

(H.32)

where by the high-pass assumption that
\widehat{\psi}_{i}=0
for all
i<s^{\star}-1
, the summation over index
i
starts from
i_{\tau}:=\max\{s^{\star}-1,\ s^{\star}-1+2\tau-s,\ \tau\}
.
It remains to analyze each
A_{\tau}
for
\tau=0,1,\ldots,s
.
To this end, isolating the dominant term with
i=i_{\tau}
and bounding the remaining terms using
|\widehat{\psi}_{i}\widehat{\psi}_{i+s-2\tau}|\leq\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}={\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,1)}[\psi^{2}(x)]
, we have

\displaystyle A_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{i_{\tau}!(i_{\tau}+s-2\tau)!}}{(i_{\tau}-\tau)!}\widehat{\psi}_{i_{\tau}}\widehat{\psi}_{i_{\tau}+s-2\tau}\pm\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{i=i_{\tau}+1}^{\infty}|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|^{i-i_{\tau}}\frac{\sqrt{i!(i+s-2\tau)!}}{(i-\tau)!}.

(H.33)

we can further simplify the second term as follows.
First note that by the definition of
i_{\tau}
,

\displaystyle\max\{i_{\tau},i_{\tau}+s-2\tau\}

\displaystyle=(s^{\star}-1)\lor(s^{\star}-1+2\tau-s)\lor\tau\lor(s^{\star}-1+s-2\tau)\lor(s^{\star}-1)\lor(s-\tau)

(H.34)

\displaystyle=(s^{\star}-1+2\tau-s)\lor(s^{\star}-1+s-2\tau)\lor\tau\lor(s-\tau)

(H.35)

\displaystyle=(s^{\star}-1+|2\tau-s|)\lor(|\tau-s/2|+s/2)

(H.36)

\displaystyle\leq s^{\star}+s-1.

(H.37)

This implies that for any
i>i_{\tau}
, we have
\max\{i,i+s-2\tau\}=i-i_{\tau}+\max\{i_{\tau},i_{\tau}+s-2\tau\}\leq s^{\star}+s-1+i-i_{\tau}
.
Therefore, for any
i\geq i_{\tau}+1
, writing
j=i-i_{\tau}-1
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{i!(i+s-2\tau)!}}{(i-\tau)!}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{i(i-1)\cdots(i-\tau+1)}\cdot\sqrt{(i+s-2\tau)(i+s-2\tau-1)\cdots(i-\tau+1)}

(H.38)

\displaystyle\leq(j+s^{\star}+s)(j+s^{\star}+s-1)\cdots(j+s^{\star}+{\lceil(s+1)/2\rceil}).

(H.39)

Applying this bound to the second term in (
H.33
), we obtain

\displaystyle A_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{i_{\tau}!(i_{\tau}+s-2\tau)!}}{(i_{\tau}-\tau)!}\widehat{\psi}_{i_{\tau}}\widehat{\psi}_{i_{\tau}+s-2\tau}\pm\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|^{j+1}\prod_{k=\lceil(s+1)/2\rceil}^{s}(j+s^{\star}+k).

(H.40)

Moreover, since
|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|\leq\epsilon_{1}\in(0,1/2)
and clearly
s^{\star}+s\geq 2{\lfloor(s+1)/2\rfloor}-1
, we can apply
Proposition

H.14
to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|^{j}\prod_{k=\lceil(s+1)/2\rceil}^{s}(j+s^{\star}+k)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{1-|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|}\prod_{k=\lceil(s+1)/2\rceil}^{s}(s^{\star}+k)\leq 4\prod_{k=\lceil(s+1)/2\rceil}^{s}(s^{\star}+k).

(H.41)

Then combining (
H.40
) and (
H.41
), we get the following decomposition for
A_{\tau}
in (
H.31
):

\displaystyle A_{\tau}=\frac{\sqrt{i_{\tau}!(i_{\tau}+s-2\tau)!}}{(i_{\tau}-\tau)!}\widehat{\psi}_{i_{\tau}}\widehat{\psi}_{i_{\tau}+s-2\tau}\pm 4\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|\prod_{k=\lceil(s+1)/2\rceil}^{s}(s^{\star}+k).

(H.42)

Further apply (
H.39
) (which is also true for
i=i_{\tau}
) to the first term above, and we have

\displaystyle|A_{\tau}|\leq 4\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}\prod_{k=\lceil(s+1)/2\rceil}^{s}(s^{\star}+k).

(H.43)

Using this bound, we can proceed to control
{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}[\psi(\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[T_{s}]]
according to (
H.31
), and we consider two cases of
s=0
and
s\geq 1
separately.

Case
s\geq 1

Expanding
\mathtt{Sym}(w^{\otimes\tau}\otimes w^{\prime\otimes(s-\tau)})[T_{s}]
in (
H.31
) by the definition of
T_{s}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\psi(\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[T_{s}]\right]\right|

(H.44)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{\tau=0}^{s}\binom{s}{\tau}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s!}}|A_{\tau}|\cdot|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|^{i_{\tau}-\tau}\cdot\left|\mathtt{Sym}\bigl(w^{\otimes\tau}\otimes{w^{\prime}}^{\otimes(s-\tau)}\bigr)[T_{s}]\right|

(H.45)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\sum_{\tau=0}^{s}\binom{s}{\tau}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s!}}|A_{\tau}|\cdot|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|^{i_{\tau}-\tau}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{s!}\sum_{\pi\in\varPi_{s}}\prod_{i=1}^{\tau}|\langle w,v_{\pi(i)}\rangle|\prod_{j=\tau+1}^{s}|\langle w^{\prime},v_{\pi(j)}\rangle|\bigg),

(H.46)

where
\varPi_{s}
denotes the set of all permutations of
[s]
.
By the assumption that
\sup_{i>c_{0}}\{|\langle w,v_{i}\rangle|\lor|\langle w^{\prime},v_{i}\rangle|\}\leq\epsilon
and the definition
\epsilon_{0}=\max_{1\leq i\leq c_{0}}\{|\langle w,v_{i}\rangle|\lor|\langle w^{\prime},v_{i}\rangle|\}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\psi(\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[T_{s}]\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\tau=0}^{s}\binom{s}{\tau}\frac{1}{\sqrt{s!}}\max_{0\leq\tau\leq s}|A_{\tau}|\cdot|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|^{i_{\tau}-\tau}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0}

(H.47)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2^{s}}{\sqrt{s!}}\max_{0\leq\tau\leq s}|A_{\tau}|\cdot|\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle|^{(s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor})\lor 0}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0}.

(H.48)

where the last inequality follows from the following fact as
i_{\tau}=\max\{s^{\star}-1,\ s^{\star}-1+2\tau-s,\ \tau\}
:

\displaystyle i_{\tau}-\tau

\displaystyle=(s^{\star}-1-\tau)\lor(s^{\star}-1+\tau-s)\lor 0=(s^{\star}-1-s/2+|\tau-s/2|)\lor 0

(H.49)

\displaystyle\geq(s^{\star}-1-s/2+\ind(s~\text{odd})/2)\lor 0=(s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor})\lor 0.

(H.50)

Next, invoking the bound (
H.43
) for
|A_{\tau}|
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\psi(\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\cdot{\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[T_{s}]\right]\right|

(H.51)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{2^{s}}{\sqrt{s!}}4\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}\prod_{k=\lceil(s+1)/2\rceil}^{s}(s^{\star}+k)\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{(s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor})\lor 0}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0}

(H.52)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\bigg(\frac{4e(s+s^{\star}-1)}{s}\bigg)^{s/2}\sqrt{s+s^{\star}}\cdot 4\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{(s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor})\lor 0}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0}

(H.53)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\left(4es^{\star}\right)^{s/2}\sqrt{s+s^{\star}}\cdot 4\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{(s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor})\lor 0}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0}.

(H.54)

Here, the second inequality follows from the fact that
s!\geq(s/e)^{s}
for all
s\geq 1
, and the last inequality holds because
s\geq 1
.

Case
s=0

For the case
s=0
, (
H.31
) simplifies to

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\psi(\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle)\right]\right|=\bigg|\sum_{i=s^{\star}-1}^{\infty}\widehat{\psi}_{i}^{2}\langle w,w^{\prime}\rangle^{i}\bigg|\leq 4\|\psi\|_{2}^{2}\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1},

(H.55)

which is also encompassed by the bound in (
H.54
) with
s=0
.
Hence, the proof is completed.
∎

Proposition H.4
.

Let
\psi:{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
satisfy
(a)
and
(b)
with
s^{\star}
being the generative exponent.
Consider any fixed
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}
and a series of test tensors
\{T_{s}\}_{s=0}^{\infty}
that satisfy the conditions in
Lemma

H.3
with constants
c_{0},\epsilon,\epsilon_{0}
and
\epsilon_{1}
specified therein, and further assume that
c_{0}\in\{0,1,2\}
and
4e\epsilon^{2}<1/2
.
Let
\zeta_{s}:{\mathbb{R}}\to{\mathbb{R}}
for
s\geq 0
be a series of functions such that there exists an absolute constant
c>0
,
{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\zeta_{s}(y)^{2}]\leq c
for all
s\geq 0
.
Then for any
s_{0}\geq 0
, the following holds:
If
s^{\star}=1
, then there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
s_{0},c_{0},c,{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\psi(y,x)^{4}]
such that

\displaystyle\sum_{s\geq s_{0}}(s+2)\cdot\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[T_{s}]\right]\right|\leq C(\epsilon_{0}^{s_{0}}+\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\epsilon^{(s_{0}-c_{0})\lor 0}).

(H.56)

In addition, if
s^{\star}\geq 2
, then it holds that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C}\sum_{s\geq s_{0}}(s+2)\cdot\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}\left[\zeta_{s}(y)\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[T_{s}]\right]\right|

(H.57)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\ind(s_{0}\leq c_{0})\cdot\left(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s_{0}/2\rfloor}}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s_{0}}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor c_{0}/2\rfloor}}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\right)+\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{(2s^{\star})\lor s_{0}-c_{0}}

(H.58)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\ind(s_{0}\leq 2(s^{\star}-1))\cdot\left(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor(c_{0}+1)/2\rfloor}}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{2(s^{\star}-1)+1-c_{0}}\right).

(H.59)

Proof of
Proposition

H.4
.

Let
F
denote the target quantity.
Invoking
Lemma

H.3
for every
s\geq s_{0}
,

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\geq s_{0}}(s+2)\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{y\sim{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[|\zeta_{s}(y)|\cdot\left|{\mathbb{E}}_{z\sim{\mathcal{N}}_{d}}\left[\psi(y,\langle w,z\rangle)\psi(y,\langle w^{\prime},z\rangle){\bm{h}}_{s}(z)[T_{s}]\right]\right|\right]

(H.60)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\geq s_{0}}(s+2)\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{y\sim{\mathbb{Q}}}\left[4|\zeta_{s}(y)|\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{x\sim{\mathcal{N}}}[\psi(y,x)^{2}]\right]\left(4es^{\star}\right)^{s/2}\sqrt{s+s^{\star}}\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{(s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor})\lor 0}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0}

(H.61)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\geq s_{0}}(s+2)\cdot 4\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)^{2}]\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\psi(y,x)^{4}]}\left(4es^{\star}\right)^{s/2}\sqrt{s+s^{\star}}\cdot\epsilon_{1}^{(s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor})\lor 0}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0},

(H.62)

where the last inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
Note that by our assumptions,
{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbb{Q}}}[\zeta_{s}(y)^{2}]\cdot{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\psi(y,x)^{4}]\leq c
uniformly over
s\geq 0
for some absolute constant
c>0
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle F\leq C\sum_{s\geq s_{0}}(s+2)\sqrt{s+s^{\star}}\left(4es^{\star}\right)^{s/2}\epsilon_{1}^{(s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor})\lor 0}\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0},

(H.63)

for a constant
C>0
.
Below the meaning of the constant
C
may change from instance to instance, but it will always depend only on
s_{0},c_{0},c,{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbb{Q}}[\psi(y,x)^{4}]
.

When
s^{\star}=1
, (
H.63
) simplifies to

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{s\geq s_{0}}(s+2)\sqrt{s+1}\left(4e\right)^{s/2}\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0}

(H.64)

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\ind(c_{0}>s_{0})\cdot\sup_{s_{0}\leq s\leq c_{0}-1}\epsilon_{0}^{s}+C\sum_{s\geq c_{0}\lor s_{0}}(s+2)\sqrt{s+1}\left(4e\right)^{s/2}\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\epsilon^{s-c_{0}}

(H.65)

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\ind(c_{0}>s_{0})\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s_{0}}+C\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\epsilon^{(s_{0}-c_{0})\lor 0}\sum_{s\geq c_{0}\lor s_{0}}(s+2)\sqrt{s+1}\left(4e\epsilon^{2}\right)^{(s-c_{0}-(s_{0}-c_{0})\vee 0)/2}

(H.66)

\displaystyle\leq C(\epsilon_{0}^{s_{0}}+\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\epsilon^{(s_{0}-c_{0})\lor 0})

(H.67)

where the last inequality holds by the assumption that
4e\epsilon^{2}<1/2
.

For the case
s^{\star}\geq 2
, we note that
c_{0}\leq 2\leq 2(s^{\star}-1)
, and thus

\displaystyle F

\displaystyle\leq C\sum_{s\geq s_{0}}(s+2)\sqrt{s+s^{\star}}\left(4es^{\star}\right)^{s/2}\epsilon_{1}^{(s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor})\lor 0}\epsilon_{0}^{s\land c_{0}}\epsilon^{(s-c_{0})\lor 0}

(H.68)

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\ind(s_{0}\leq c_{0})\cdot\max_{s_{0}\leq s\leq c_{0}}\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s}+C\cdot\ind(s_{0}\leq 2(s^{\star}-1))\cdot\max_{c_{0}+1\leq s\leq 2(s^{\star}-1)+1}\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s/2\rfloor}}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{s-c_{0}}

(H.69)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+C\sum_{s\geq(2(s^{\star}-1)+2)\lor s_{0}}(s+2)\sqrt{s+s^{\star}}\left(4es^{\star}\right)^{s/2}\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\epsilon^{s-c_{0}}

(H.70)

\displaystyle\leq C\cdot\ind(s_{0}\leq c_{0})\cdot\left(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor s_{0}/2\rfloor}}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{s_{0}}+\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor c_{0}/2\rfloor}}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\right)+C\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{(2(s^{\star}-1)+2)\lor s_{0}-c_{0}}

(H.71)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+C\cdot\ind(s_{0}\leq 2(s^{\star}-1))\cdot\left(\epsilon_{1}^{s^{\star}-1-{\lfloor(c_{0}+1)/2\rfloor}}\cdot\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon+\epsilon_{0}^{c_{0}}\cdot\epsilon^{2(s^{\star}-1)+1-c_{0}}\right)

(H.72)

where the last inequality follows from the same argument as in the previous case.
This completes the proof.
∎

H.2
Technical Results for Uniform Distribution on the Sphere

Lemma H.5

(Moment of polynomial on a sphere, adapted from
28
)
.

Let
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{d})^{\top}\sim{\text{Unif}}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
and
s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{d}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
.
Denote
s=\sum_{i=1}^{d}s_{i}
.
Then we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]=\begin{cases}0&\text{if some $s_{i}$ is odd},\\
{\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(d/2)}{\Gamma((s+d)/2)}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\Gamma((s_{i}+1)/2)}{\Gamma(1/2)}}&\text{if all $s_{i}$ are even},\end{cases}

(H.73)

where
\Gamma
is the gamma function.

To evaluate the formula given by the above lemma, we provide the following bound.

Lemma H.6
.

Let
s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{d}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
be even integers and denote
s=\sum_{i=1}^{d}s_{i}
.
Then

\displaystyle\left(\frac{1}{d+s}\right)^{s/2}\leq\frac{\Gamma(d/2)}{\Gamma((s+d)/2)}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\Gamma((s_{i}+1)/2)}{\Gamma(1/2)}\leq\left(\frac{s}{d}\right)^{s/2},

(H.74)

where we adopt the convention
(0)^{0}=1
for the case of
s=0
.

Proof of
Lemma

H.6
.

The gamma function satisfies that
\Gamma(a+1)=a\Gamma(a)
for any
a>0
.
Applying this fact, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\Gamma(d/2)}{\Gamma((s+d)/2)}\prod_{i=1}^{d}\frac{\Gamma((s_{i}+1)/2)}{\Gamma(1/2)}=\frac{\prod_{i=1}^{d}(s_{i}-1)!!}{(s+d-2)(s+d-4)\cdots d}.

(H.75)

The lower bound immediately follows from the fact that
\prod_{i=1}^{d}(s_{i}-1)!!\geq 1
and that all
s/2
terms in the denominator are at most
s+d
.
For the upper bound, it suffices to lower bound each of the
s/2
terms in the denominator by
d
and upper bound each of the
s/2
terms in the numerator by
s
.
∎

Note that the second moment
{\mathbb{E}}[\xi_{i}^{2}]\asymp d^{-1}
, thus
Lemma

H.6
can be viewed as some kind of
reverse Holder’s inequality
where we use lower moment to control higher moment.
Notably, the reverse inequality gives a
dimension-free
bound for the moments of the polynomial on the sphere.
The following proposition formalizes this intuition.

Proposition H.7
.

Let
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{d})^{\top}\sim{\text{Unif}}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
.
For any
\varepsilon>0
, let
f:{\mathbb{R}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{R}}
be a function such that

{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi}[(f(\xi)-1)^{2}]\leq\varepsilon^{2}
.
Let
s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{d}\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
be even integers and denote
s=\sum_{i=1}^{d}s_{i}
.
Then it holds that

\displaystyle\bigg(1-\bigg(\frac{2s(d+s)}{d}\bigg)^{s/2}\varepsilon\bigg)\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{d+s}\bigg)^{s/2}\leq{\mathbb{E}}_{\xi}\bigg[f(\xi)\prod_{i=1}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]\leq(1+2^{s/2}\varepsilon)\cdot\left(\frac{s}{d}\right)^{s/2}.

(H.76)

Proof of
Proposition

H.7
.

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\bigg|{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[(f(\xi)-1)\prod_{i=1}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]\bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}[(f(\xi)-1)^{2}]}\cdot\sqrt{{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{d}\xi_{i}^{2s_{i}}\bigg]}\leq\varepsilon\cdot\left(\frac{2s}{d}\right)^{s/2},

(H.77)

where we use
Lemma

H.5
and the upper bound in
Lemma

H.6
for the second inequality.
Then the proof is completed by incorporating the lower and upper bounds for
{\mathbb{E}}[\prod_{i=1}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}]
from
Lemma

H.6
.
∎

Proposition H.8
.

Let
\xi=(\xi_{1},\ldots,\xi_{d})^{\top}\sim{\text{Unif}}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
.
Let
{\bm{s}}=(s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{d})^{\top}
where
s_{1},\ldots,s_{d}
are non-negative integers, and denote
s=\sum_{i=1}^{d}s_{i}
.
Then for any fixed
\gamma>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\left(\xi_{1}+\gamma\right)^{s_{1}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if $s_{i}$ is odd for some $2\leq i\leq d$},\\
C(\bm{s},\gamma)\cdot\gamma^{\ind(s_{1}~\text{odd})}\big(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\big)^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\big)^{s-s_{1}},&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(H.78)

where
C({\bm{s}},\gamma)
satisfies that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{5}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{d+s}}\bigg)^{s-\ind(s_{1}\text{ odd})}\leq C(\bm{s},\gamma)\leq s^{s/2}.

(H.79)

Proof of
Proposition

H.8
.

For convenience, we denote the target quantity by
G({\bm{s}})
.
Expanding
(\xi_{1}+\gamma)^{s_{1}}
, we have

\displaystyle G(\bm{s}):={\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\left(\xi_{1}+\gamma\right)^{s_{1}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]={\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\sum_{j=0}^{s_{1}}\binom{s_{1}}{j}\xi_{1}^{j}\gamma^{s_{1}-j}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg].

(H.80)

Note that if there exists any odd
s_{i}
for
i\geq 2
, then
G(\bm{s})=0
due to
Lemma

H.5
.
It remains to consider the case where all
s_{i}
for
i\geq 2
are even, and by the same symmetry only the terms with even powers of
\xi_{1}
contribute.
Applying
Lemma

H.5
, we get

\displaystyle G(\bm{s})

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\binom{s_{1}}{2j}\gamma^{s_{1}-2j}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\xi_{1}^{2j}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

(H.81)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\binom{s_{1}}{2j}\gamma^{s_{1}-2j}\cdot\frac{\Gamma(d/2)}{\Gamma((s-s_{1}+2j+d)/2)}\cdot\frac{\Gamma(j+1/2)}{\Gamma(1/2)}\cdot\prod_{i=2}^{d}\frac{\Gamma((s_{i}+1)/2)}{\Gamma(1/2)}

(H.82)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\binom{s_{1}}{2j}\gamma^{s_{1}-2j}\cdot\underbrace{\frac{(2j-1)!!\cdot\prod_{i=2}^{d}(s_{i}-1)!!}{(s-s_{1}+2j+d-2)\cdot(s-s_{1}+2j+d-4)\cdots d}}_{\displaystyle\rm(I)},

(H.83)

where the third equality follows from the property of the gamma function that
\Gamma(a+1)=a\Gamma(a)
for any
a>0
.
Below we derive the lower and upper bounds of
G(\bm{s})
separately.

Lower Bound

For the lower bound of
G({\bm{s}})
, we lower bound the numerator in (I) by 1 and upper bound each of the
(s-s_{1}+2j)/2
terms in the denominator by
d+s
.
This yields

\displaystyle G({\bm{s}})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{j=0}^{{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\binom{s_{1}}{2j}\gamma^{s_{1}-2j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d+s}}\bigg)^{2j+s-s_{1}}\geq\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{d+s}}\bigg)^{s-\ind(s_{1}\text{ odd})}\sum_{j=0}^{{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\binom{s_{1}}{2j}\gamma^{s_{1}-2j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{2j+s-s_{1}}

(H.84)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{d+s}}\bigg)^{s-\ind(s_{1}\text{ odd})}\sum_{j=0}^{{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\binom{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}{2j}\gamma^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}-2j}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{2j}\cdot\gamma^{\ind(s_{1}~\text{odd})}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}

(H.85)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{d+s}}\bigg)^{s-\ind(s_{1}\text{ odd})}\sum_{j=0,\ j\text{ even}}^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\binom{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}{j}\gamma^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}-j}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{j}\cdot\gamma^{\ind(s_{1}~\text{odd})}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}

(H.86)

where the third equality is true because
s_{1}\geq 2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}
.
Next we further relate the sum over even
j
to the sum over all
j=0,1,\ldots,2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}
.
To this end, we will apply
Lemma

H.13
by defining

\displaystyle A_{j}=\binom{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}{j}\cdot\gamma^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}-j}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{j}.

(H.87)

Then invoking
Lemma

H.13
with
a=\gamma
and
b=1/\sqrt{d}
, we get

\displaystyle A_{j}\leq 2(A_{j-1}+A_{j+1}),\quad j=1,3,\ldots,2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}-1.

(H.88)

Therefore, the summation of
A_{j}
over all odd
j=1,3,\ldots,2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}-1
can be upper bounded by 4 times the summation of
A_{j}
over all even
j=0,2,\ldots,2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}
.
Applying this to (
H.86
) yields

\displaystyle G(\bm{s})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{5}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{d+s}}\bigg)^{s-\ind(s_{1}\text{ odd})}\sum_{j=0}^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\binom{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}{j}\gamma^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}-j}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{j}\cdot\gamma^{\ind(s_{1}~\text{odd})}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}

(H.89)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{5}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{d+s}}\bigg)^{s-\ind(s_{1}\text{ odd})}\cdot\left(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\cdot\gamma^{\ind(s_{1}~\text{odd})}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{s-s_{1}}.

(H.90)

This gives the lower bound of
G(\bm{s})
.

Upper Bound

For the upper bound of
G({\bm{s}})
, we upper bound the numerator in (I) by
s^{s/2}
and lower bound each of the
s/2
terms in the denominator by
d
.
This yields

\displaystyle G(\bm{s})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\binom{s_{1}}{2j}\gamma^{s_{1}-2j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{2j+s-s_{1}}\cdot s^{s/2}

(H.91)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=0}^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\binom{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}{j}\gamma^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}-j}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}\cdot\gamma^{\ind(s_{1}~\text{odd})}\cdot s^{s/2}

(H.92)

\displaystyle=s^{s/2}\cdot\left(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\cdot\gamma^{\ind(s_{1}~\text{odd})}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{s-s_{1}},

(H.93)

where the second inequality holds since
\gamma>0
.
Finally, combining the lower bound in (
H.90
) and the upper bound in (
H.93
), we conclude that

\displaystyle G(\bm{s})=C(\bm{s},\gamma)\cdot\left(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{2{\lfloor s_{1}/2\rfloor}}\cdot\gamma^{\ind(s_{1}~\text{odd})}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{s-s_{1}}

(H.94)

for
(\sqrt{d}/\sqrt{d+s})^{s-\ind(s_{1}\text{ odd})}/5\leq C(\bm{s},\gamma)\leq s^{s/2}
, when
s_{2},\ldots,s_{d}
are even, completing the proof.
∎

H.3
Technical Results on Polarized Random Vectors

Lemma H.9

(Moments of weakly polarized random vector)
.

Let
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\ldots,\xi_{d})^{\top}\sim{\text{Unif}}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
and
e_{1}=(1,0,\ldots,0)
be the first canonical basis vector in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
For a fixed integer
s\geq 1
, consider
{\bm{s}}=(s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{d})^{\top}
where
s_{1},s_{2},\ldots,s_{d}
are non-negative integers such that
s=\sum_{i=1}^{d}s_{i}
.
For any fixed polarization level
\gamma\in(0,1)
, define the polarized vector
w
as

\displaystyle w=\frac{\xi+\gamma e_{1}}{\|\xi+\gamma e_{1}\|_{2}},

(H.95)

where
e_{1}=(1,0,\ldots,0)^{\top}
is the first canonical basis vector in
{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
Then it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{d}w_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if some $s_{i}$ is odd for $i=2,3,\ldots,d$},\\
C(\bm{s},\gamma)\cdot\left(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{s_{1}}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{s-s_{1}},&\text{if $s_{1}$ is even and $s_{2},\ldots,s_{d}$ are even},\\
C(\bm{s},\gamma)\cdot\gamma^{\ind(s_{1}\text{ odd})}\big(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\big)^{s_{1}-1}\big(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\big)^{s-s_{1}},&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}

(H.96)

where
C(\bm{s},\gamma)
satisfies that

\displaystyle\frac{s^{(s-1)/2}}{5(1+\gamma)^{s}}\lesssim C(\bm{s},\gamma)\leq\frac{s^{s/2}}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}.

(H.97)

Proof of
Lemma

H.9
.

Let
r:=\|\xi+\gamma e_{1}\|_{2}
, and denote the target quantity by
G(w,{\bm{s}})
for convenience.
Then we can rewrite
G(w,{\bm{s}})
as

\displaystyle G(w,{\bm{s}})={\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\prod_{i=1}^{d}w_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{\left(\xi_{1}+\gamma\right)^{s_{1}}}{r^{s}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg].

(H.98)

By symmetry, we have
G(w,{\bm{s}})=0
if some
s_{i}
is odd for
i=2,3,\ldots,d
.
It remains to consider the case where
s_{2},s_{3},\ldots,s_{d}
are all even, and we still study the case for
s_{1}
being even and odd separately.

Case 1:
s_{1}
is even

We first consider the simpler case where
s_{1}
is even.
In this case,
(\xi_{1}+\gamma)^{s_{1}},\xi_{2}^{s_{2}},\ldots,\xi_{d}^{s_{d}}
are all non-negative.
Also, by triangle inequality we have
r\geq 1-\gamma
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle G(w,{\bm{s}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[(\xi_{1}+\gamma)^{s_{1}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]=\frac{C({\bm{s}},\gamma)}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}

(H.99)

where
C({\bm{s}},\gamma)
is given by
Proposition

H.8
.
Similarly, since
r\leq 1+\gamma
, we also have

\displaystyle G(w,{\bm{s}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C({\bm{s}},\gamma)}{(1+\gamma)^{s}}\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}.

(H.100)

Combining the above two inequalities gives the desired result for the case where
s_{1}
is even.

Case 2:
s_{1}
is odd

We now consider the more complicated case where
s_{1}
is odd.
We first rewrite
G(w,{\bm{s}})
as follows

\displaystyle G(w,{\bm{s}})

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{\left(\xi_{1}+\gamma\right)^{s_{1}}}{r^{s}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

(H.101)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\sum_{j=0}^{(s_{1}-1)/2}\binom{s_{1}}{2j}\gamma^{s_{1}-2j}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{\xi_{1}^{2j}}{r^{s}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]}_{\displaystyle\rm(I)~even~terms}+\underbrace{\sum_{j=0}^{(s_{1}-1)/2}\binom{s_{1}}{2j+1}\gamma^{s_{1}-2j-1}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\left[\frac{\xi_{1}^{2j+1}}{r^{s}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\right]}_{\displaystyle\rm(II)~odd~terms}.

(H.102)

Let us first look at the odd terms of (
H.101
).
Denote
r_{+}:=\|\xi+\gamma e_{1}\|_{2}
and
r_{-}:=\|-\xi+\gamma e_{1}\|_{2}
.
By symmetry, each of the odd terms in (
H.101
) can be written as

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{\xi_{1}^{2j+1}}{r^{s}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\left(\frac{1}{r_{+}^{s}}-\frac{1}{r_{-}^{s}}\right)\xi_{1}^{2j+1}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]=\frac{1}{2}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{(r_{-}^{2}-r_{+}^{2})\sum_{l=0}^{s-1}r_{-}^{l}r_{+}^{s-1-l}}{r_{+}^{s}\cdot r_{-}^{s}\cdot(r_{+}+r_{-})}\cdot\xi_{1}^{2j+1}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

(H.103)

\displaystyle=-\gamma\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{2\sum_{l=0}^{s-1}r_{-}^{l}r_{+}^{s-1-l}}{r_{+}^{s}\cdot r_{-}^{s}\cdot(r_{+}+r_{-})}\cdot\xi_{1}^{2j+2}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

(H.104)

where the last equality holds by noting that
r_{-}^{2}-r_{+}^{2}=-4\gamma\xi_{1}
.
Since both
r_{+},r_{-}\geq 1-\gamma
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\frac{\frac{1}{s}\sum_{l=0}^{s-1}2r_{-}^{l}r_{+}^{s-1-l}}{r_{+}^{s}r_{-}^{s}(r_{+}+r_{-})}\leq\sup_{\xi\in\mathbb{S}^{d-1}}\max_{l=0,1,\ldots,s-1}\frac{1}{r_{+}^{l+1}r_{-}^{s-l}(r_{+}+r_{-})/2}\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}.

(H.105)

Applying the above bound to (
H.104
), and noting that every quantity inside the expectation on the right-hand side is non-negative, we obtain that for every
j=0,1,\ldots,(s_{1}-1)/2
,

\displaystyle-{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{\xi_{1}^{2j+1}}{r^{s}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{s\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\xi_{1}^{2j+2}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

(H.106)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{s\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{s-s_{1}+2j+2}{d}\bigg)^{(s-s_{1}+2j+2)/2}

(H.107)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{s\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}\cdot\bigg(\frac{s+1}{d}\bigg)^{(s-s_{1}+2j+2)/2}

(H.108)

Collecting the above upper bound for all the odd terms in (
H.101
), we have

\displaystyle-\text{(II)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{s\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}\sum_{j=0}^{(s_{1}-1)/2}\binom{s_{1}}{2j+1}\cdot\gamma^{s_{1}-2j-1}\cdot\left(\frac{s+1}{d}\right)^{(s-s_{1}+2j+2)/2}

(H.109)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{s_{1}s\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}\left(\frac{s+1}{d}\right)^{(s-s_{1}+2)/2}\sum_{j=0}^{(s_{1}-1)/2}\binom{s_{1}-1}{2j}\cdot\gamma^{s_{1}-1-2j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{s+1}{d}\bigg)^{j}

(H.110)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{s_{1}s\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}\left(\frac{s+1}{d}\right)^{(s-s_{1}+2)/2}\sum_{j=0}^{s_{1}-1}\binom{s_{1}-1}{j}\cdot\gamma^{s_{1}-1-j}\cdot\bigg(\frac{s+1}{d}\bigg)^{j/2}

(H.111)

where the second inequality holds because
\binom{s_{1}}{2j+1}\leq\binom{s_{1}-1}{2j}\cdot s_{1}
for all
j=0,1,\ldots,(s_{1}-1)/2
.
Thus, we get the following lower bound for
(II)
:

\displaystyle\text{(II)}\geq-\frac{s_{1}s\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}\bigg(\gamma+\frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}+2}.

(H.112)

Next, we study the even terms (I) in (
H.101
).
Similar to the analysis for the case of even
s_{1}
, applying
Lemma

H.6
yields the following lower and upper bounds for every
j=0,1,\ldots,(s_{1}-1)/2
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{\xi_{1}^{2j}}{r^{s}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\xi_{1}^{2j}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}\bigg(\frac{s-1}{d}\bigg)^{(s-s_{1}+2j)/2},

(H.113)

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{\xi_{1}^{2j}}{r^{s}}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{(1+\gamma)^{s}}\cdot{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\xi_{1}^{2j}\prod_{i=2}^{d}\xi_{i}^{s_{i}}\bigg]\geq\frac{1}{(1+\gamma)^{s}}\bigg(\frac{1}{d+s-1}\bigg)^{(s-s_{1}+2j)/2}

(H.114)

Collecting the upper bound for all the even terms in (
H.101
), we have

(I)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}\sum_{j=0}^{(s_{1}-1)/2}\binom{s_{1}-1}{2j}\cdot\gamma^{s_{1}-2j}\cdot\left(\frac{s-1}{d}\right)^{(s-s_{1}+2j)/2}

(H.115)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{s_{1}\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}\left(\frac{s-1}{d}\right)^{(s-s_{1})/2}\sum_{j=0}^{s_{1}-1}\binom{s_{1}-1}{j}\cdot\gamma^{s_{1}-1-j}\cdot\left(\frac{s-1}{d}\right)^{j/2}

(H.116)

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{1}\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}\bigg(\gamma+\frac{\sqrt{s-1}}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\left(\frac{\sqrt{s-1}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{s-s_{1}},

(H.117)

where the second inequality is because
\binom{s_{1}}{2j}\leq\binom{s_{1}-1}{j}\cdot s_{1}
for all
j=0,1,\ldots,(s_{1}-1)/2
.
Similarly, collecting the lower bound for all
j=0,1,\ldots,(s_{1}-1)/2
, we have

(I)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{(1+\gamma)^{s}}\sum_{j=0}^{(s_{1}-1)/2}\binom{s_{1}-1}{2j}\cdot\gamma^{s_{1}-2j}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{d+s-1}\right)^{(s-s_{1}+2j)/2}

(H.118)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\gamma}{5(1+\gamma)^{s}}\left(\frac{1}{d+s-1}\right)^{(s-s_{1})/2}\sum_{j=0}^{s_{1}-1}\binom{s_{1}-1}{j}\cdot\gamma^{s_{1}-1-j}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{d+s-1}\right)^{j/2}

(H.119)

\displaystyle=\frac{\gamma}{5(1+\gamma)^{s}}\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d+s-1}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d+s-1}}\right)^{s-s_{1}},

(H.120)

where the second inequality follows from the same argument as the analysis for (
H.86
).

Since
\text{II}\leq 0
by (
H.104
), it follows from the upper bound for (I) in (
H.117
) that

\displaystyle G(w,{\bm{s}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{s_{1}\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}\bigg(\gamma+\frac{\sqrt{s-1}}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\left(\frac{\sqrt{s-1}}{\sqrt{d}}\right)^{s-s_{1}}

(H.121)

\displaystyle=\frac{s_{1}}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}\bigg(\frac{\gamma\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{s-1}}{\gamma\sqrt{d}+1}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}(s-1)^{(s-s_{1})/2}\cdot\gamma\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}

(H.122)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{s^{s/2}}{(1-\gamma)^{s}}\cdot\gamma\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}.

(H.123)

Combining the lower bound for (I) in (
H.120
) and the lower bound for (II) in (
H.112
), we have

\displaystyle G(w,{\bm{s}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\gamma}{5(1+\gamma)^{s}}\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d+s-1}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d+s-1}}\right)^{s-s_{1}}

(H.124)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{s_{1}s\gamma}{(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}\cdot\bigg(\gamma+\frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\cdot\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{s+1}}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}+2}

(H.125)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{5(1+\gamma)^{s}}\bigg(\frac{(\gamma\sqrt{d+s-1}+1)\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{d+s-1}(\gamma\sqrt{d}+1)}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{d+s-1}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}\cdot\gamma\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}

(H.126)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{s_{1}s}{(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}\bigg(\frac{\gamma\sqrt{d}+\sqrt{s-1}}{\gamma\sqrt{d}+1}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}(s+1)^{(s-s_{1})/2}\frac{s+1}{d}\cdot\gamma\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}

(H.127)

\displaystyle\geq\bigg[\frac{s^{(s-1)/2}}{5(1+\gamma)^{s}}-\frac{s^{2}(s+1)^{(s+1)/2}}{d(1-\gamma)^{s+2}}\bigg]\cdot\gamma\bigg(\gamma+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s_{1}-1}\bigg(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\bigg)^{s-s_{1}}.

(H.128)

Combining (
H.123
) and (
H.128
) gives the desired result.
∎

H.4
Auxiliary Lemmas

Lemma H.10

(Bernstein’s inequality)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
be independent random variables with
|X_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}[X_{i}]|\leq C
for all
i\in[n]
.
Then for any
t>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}X_{i}\bigg|\geq t\bigg)\leq 2\exp\bigg(-\frac{nt^{2}/2}{n^{-1}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Var[X_{i}]+Ct/3}\bigg),

(H.129)

or equivalently, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\bigg(\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}X_{i}\bigg|\leq\sqrt{\frac{2\cdot n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Var[X_{i}]\cdot\log\delta^{-1}}{n}}+\frac{C\log\delta^{-1}}{3n}\bigg)\geq 1-\delta.

(H.130)

For the vector case, by a union bound over all the coordinates, we have the following corollary.

Corollary H.11

(Vector version of Bernstein’s inequality)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}
be independent random vectors in
