{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
with
\|X_{i}-{\mathbb{E}}[X_{i}]\|_{\infty}\leq C
for all
i\in[n]
.
Then for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, it holds with probability at least
1-\delta
that

\displaystyle\bigg\|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\bigg\|_{2}\lesssim\bigg\|{\mathbb{E}}\bigg[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}\bigg]\bigg\|_{2}+\sqrt{\frac{n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}(\Cov[X_{i}])\cdot\log(d\delta^{-1})}{n}}+\frac{\sqrt{d}C\log(d\delta^{-1})}{n}.

(H.131)

Lemma H.12

(Lemma I.3. in
17
)
.

Let
X_{1},\ldots,X_{n}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
be independent mean-zero random vectors such that for all
p\geq 2
,
{\mathbb{E}}[\|X_{i}\|^{p}]^{1/p}\leq Cp^{k/2}
for some constants
k,C\geq 0
and vector norm
\|\cdot\|
.
Define
\sigma^{2}:=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\mathbb{E}}[\|X_{i}\|^{2}]
and
Y:=n^{-1}\cdot\sum_{i=1}^{n}X_{i}
.
Then with probability at least
1-2\delta
,

\displaystyle\|Y\|\lesssim\sigma\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{C\log(1/\delta)\log(n/\delta)^{k/2}}{n}.

(H.132)

where
\lesssim
only hides constant that depends on
k
.

Lemma H.13

(Ratio bound of binomial expansion)
.

For real numbers
a,b>0
and integer
s\geq 2
, define

\displaystyle A_{j}:=\binom{s}{j}\cdot a^{s-j}\cdot b^{j},\quad\text{for }j=0,1,\ldots,s.

(H.133)

Then for all
j=1,2,\ldots,s-1
, it holds that
A_{j}\leq 2(A_{j-1}+A_{j+1})
.

Proof of
Lemma

H.13
.

By the definition of
A_{j}
,

\displaystyle\min\left\{\frac{A_{j}}{A_{j-1}},\frac{A_{j}}{A_{j+1}}\right\}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\frac{b}{a}\cdot\frac{s-j+1}{j},\frac{a}{b}\cdot\frac{j+1}{s-j}\right\}\leq\sqrt{\frac{b}{a}\cdot\frac{s-j+1}{j}\cdot\frac{a}{b}\cdot\frac{j+1}{s-j}}

(H.134)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\left(1+\frac{1}{s-j}\right)\left(1+\frac{1}{j}\right)}\leq 2.

(H.135)

Hence, the proof is complete by the nonnegativity of
A_{j}
.
∎

Proposition H.14
.

For
\epsilon\in[0,1/2)
and
s,r\in{\mathbb{N}}_{0}
with
s\geq 2r-1
, it holds that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(j+s)(j+s-1)\cdots(j+s-r+1)\cdot\epsilon^{j}\leq 2s\cdot(s-1)\cdots(s-r+1)\cdot\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}.

(H.136)

Proof of
Proposition

H.14
.

Denote
F(x)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}(j+s)(j+s-1)\cdots(j+s-r+1)x^{j}
for
x\in(0,1)
.
The desired quantity on the left-hand side of the inequality is simply
F(\epsilon)
.
It can be verified using the expansion of
1/(1-x)=\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}x^{j}
for
x\in(0,1)
that

\displaystyle F(x)=\frac{{\mathrm{d}}^{r}}{{\mathrm{d}}x^{r}}\Big(\frac{x^{s}}{1-x}\Big)\cdot x^{-(s-r)}.

(H.137)

Expanding this expression, we have

\displaystyle F(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau=0}^{r}\binom{r}{\tau}\frac{{\mathrm{d}}^{\tau}}{{\mathrm{d}}x^{\tau}}\left(x^{s}\right)\cdot\frac{{\mathrm{d}}^{r-\tau}}{{\mathrm{d}}x^{r-\tau}}\left(\frac{1}{1-x}\right)\cdot x^{-(s-r)}

(H.138)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau=0}^{r}\binom{r}{\tau}s(s-1)\cdots(s-\tau+1)\cdot x^{s-\tau}\cdot\frac{(-1)^{r-\tau}(r-\tau)!}{(1-x)^{r-\tau+1}}\cdot x^{-(s-r)}

(H.139)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau=0}^{r}\left(r\cdot(r-1)\cdots(\tau+1)\right)\cdot\left(s\cdot(s-1)\cdots(s-\tau+1)\right)\cdot(-1)^{r-\tau}\cdot\frac{x^{r-\tau}}{(1-x)^{r-\tau+1}}.

(H.140)

Write the above summation as
F(x)=\sum_{\tau=0}^{r}F_{\tau}(x)
, where
F_{\tau}(x)
is the
\tau
-th term in the summation.
Then for each
\tau=0,\ldots,r-1
, when
x\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{F_{\tau}(x)}{F_{\tau+1}(x)}=-\frac{s-\tau}{\tau+1}\cdot\frac{1-x}{x}<-1.

(H.141)

Note that
F_{r}(x)
is positive, and for each
k=1,\ldots,\lfloor r/2\rfloor
,
F_{r-2k+1}(x)<-F_{r-2k}(x)<0
.
Since
F(x)
is positive, it is then upper bounded by
F_{\tau}(x)+|F_{0}(x)|
, i.e.,

\displaystyle F(x)

\displaystyle\leq s\cdot(s-1)\cdots(s-r+1)\cdot\frac{1}{1-x}+r!\cdot\frac{x^{r}}{(1-x)^{r+1}}\leq 2s\cdot(s-1)\cdots(s-r+1)\cdot\frac{1}{1-x}.

(H.142)

The proof is complete by setting
x=\epsilon
.
∎

Lemma H.15

(Gaussian-like tail bound for spherical coordinate)
.

Suppose
\xi\sim{\text{Unif}}({\mathbb{S}}^{d-1})
.
Then the first coordinate of
\xi
, denoted by
\xi_{1}
, satisfies that for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\Pr(\xi_{1}\geq\sqrt{Cd^{-1}\log d})\leq\exp(-d/16)+d^{-C/4},

(H.143)

where
C>0
is a constant.

Proof of
Lemma

H.15
.

Consider
z\sim{\mathcal{N}}(0,I_{d})
, and it holds that
\xi_{1}\overset{d}{=}z_{1}/\|z\|_{2}
, where
z_{1}
is the first coordinate of
z
.
Note that
\|z\|_{2}^{2}\sim\chi^{2}_{d}
, and by a standard tail bound for the
\chi^{2}_{d}
distribution, we have

\displaystyle\Pr(\|z\|_{2}^{2}\leq d-2\sqrt{dx})\leq\exp(-x),\quad\text{for any }x\geq 0.

(H.144)

By taking
x=d/16
, we get
\Pr(\|z\|_{2}^{2}\leq d/2)\leq\exp(-d/16)
.
Thus, applying a union bound,

\displaystyle\Pr(\xi_{1}\geq t)

\displaystyle=\Pr\Big(\frac{z_{1}}{\|z\|_{2}}\geq t\Big)\leq\Pr\left(\|z\|_{2}^{2}\leq d/2\right)+\Pr(z_{1}\geq t\sqrt{d/2})

(H.145)

\displaystyle\leq\exp(-d/16)+\exp(-t^{2}d/4).

(H.146)

The proof is complete by taking
t=\sqrt{Cd^{-1}\log d}
.
∎

Lemma H.16

(Hypergeometric moment and tail bound)
.

Consider random variable
X\sim\mathrm{Hypergeometric}(d,k,k)
with probability mass

\displaystyle\Pr(X=x)=\frac{\binom{k}{x}\binom{d-k}{k-x}}{\binom{d}{k}},\quad\text{for }x=1,2,\ldots k.

(H.147)

Suppose
k=o(\sqrt{d})
, then for any constant
s>0
, it holds that
{\mathbb{E}}[X^{s}]\simeq k^{2}/d
.
In addition, the following tail bound holds:

\displaystyle\Pr(X\geq\log k)\lesssim(k^{2}/d)^{\log k}.

(H.148)

Proof of
Lemma

H.16
.

We first notice that for
x\geq k^{2}/d
, since
k=o(\sqrt{d})
,

\displaystyle\frac{\Pr(X=x+1)}{\Pr(X=x)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(k-x)^{2}}{(x+1)(d-2k-x+1)}=\Big(1-\frac{k}{d}\Big)^{2}\Big/\Big(\frac{d}{k^{2}}+o\Big(\frac{d}{k^{2}}\Big)\Big)\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{d}.

(H.149)

This immediately implies the tail bound:

\displaystyle\Pr(X\geq\log k)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=\lceil\log k\rceil}^{k}\Pr(X=j)\lesssim\Big(\frac{k^{2}}{d}\Big)^{\log k}.

(H.150)

Next, for the moment
{\mathbb{E}}[X^{s}]
, we first study the magnitude of
{\mathbb{P}}(X=0)
, which, by Stirling’s approximation, is given by

\displaystyle\Pr(X=0)

\displaystyle=\frac{((d-k)!)^{2}}{d!\cdot(d-2k)!}\simeq\frac{(d-k)^{2(d-k)+1}\cdot e^{-2(d-k)}}{d^{d+1/2}\cdot(d-2k)^{(d-2k)+1/2}\cdot e^{-2(d-k)}}

(H.151)

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-2k/d+k^{2}/d^{2})^{d-k+1/2}}{(1-2k/d)^{d-2k+1/2}}

(H.152)

\displaystyle=\bigg(1+\frac{1}{(1-2k/d)\cdot d^{2}/k^{2}}\bigg)^{d-k+1/2}\bigg(1-\frac{2k}{d}\bigg)^{k}.

(H.153)

Since
k=o(\sqrt{d})
, we have
(1-2k/d)^{k}\simeq 1
.
Further applying the fact that
(1+1/m)^{m}=\Theta(1)
,

\displaystyle\Pr(X=0)

\displaystyle\simeq\bigg(1+\frac{1}{(1-2k/d)\cdot d^{2}/k^{2}}\bigg)^{(1-2k/d)\cdot d^{2}/k^{2}\cdot k^{2}/d}=\Theta(1).

(H.154)

As a consequence, using the first equality in (
H.149
), we can lower bound the expectation of
X^{s}
by

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[X^{s}]

\displaystyle\geq\Pr(X=1)=\Pr(X=0)\cdot\frac{k^{2}}{d-2k+1}\gtrsim\frac{k^{2}}{d}.

(H.155)

For the upper bound, we again use the first equality in (
H.149
) to get

\displaystyle\Pr(X=x+1)\leq\frac{k^{2}}{(x+1)(d-2k-x+1)}\cdot\Pr(X=x)\lesssim\frac{\Pr(X=x)}{x+1}\cdot\frac{k^{2}}{d}.

(H.156)

Recursive application of this inequality yields that
{\mathbb{P}}(X=x)\lesssim{\mathbb{P}}(X=0)\cdot(k^{2}/d)^{x}/x!
, and thus

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[X^{s}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{x=1}^{k}x^{s}\cdot\Pr(X=x)\leq\Pr(X=0)\cdot\sum_{x=1}^{k}\frac{x^{s}}{x!}\Big(\frac{k^{2}}{d}\Big)^{x}\lesssim\frac{k^{2}}{d}.

(H.157)

Therefore, we conclude that
{\mathbb{E}}[X^{s}]\simeq k^{2}/d
.
This completes the proof.
∎
